{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KrsRW9ho4M7PeWN7WQk7jv","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→#!/usr/bin/env python3\n     2→\"\"\"Convert the Brain-wide Activity Map dataset (DANDI 000363) to the decoder format.\n     3→\n     4→    python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full | --sample] [--datadir DIR]\n     5→\"\"\"\n     6→\n     7→import argparse\n     8→import glob\n     9→import os\n    10→import pickle\n    11→import re\n    12→import sys\n    13→import time\n    14→\n    15→import numpy as np\n    16→from pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\n    17→\n    18→_chen2024_seed = 'x5cidj2hy87s'\n    19→np.random.seed(sum(ord(c) for c in _chen2024_seed) % 2**31)\n    20→\n    21→HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\n    22→# Default only, for running this by hand from manual/chen2024/. The oracle arm runs\n    23→# the script from /solution while the dataset is mounted read-only at /app/data, so\n    24→# solve.sh overrides this with --datadir.\n    25→DATA_DIR = os.path.join(HERE, 'data')\n    26→\n    27→# trial window relative to the go cue, and bin width, in seconds\n    28→T_START, T_STOP = -2.5, 1.5\n    29→BIN = 0.05\n    30→N_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))             # 80\n    31→\n    32→REL_EDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)         # 81 edges, re go cue\n    33→CENTERS = REL_EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                        # 80 bin centres\n    34→\n    35→OUTCOME_CODE = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n    36→EARLY_CODE = {'no early': 0, 'early': 1}\n    37→SIDE_CODE = {'left': 0, 'right': 1}\n    38→CHOICE_NO_LICK = 2               # no lick in the response window\n    39→TONGUE_HIDDEN = 3                # no visible tongue frame in the bin\n    40→\n    41→TONGUE_CONF = 0.5                # tracking likelihood for the tongue to count as visible\n    42→TONGUE_PCT = (40, 60)            # per-session percentiles -> 3 visible classes\n    43→\n    44→INPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_tone_onset', 'photostim']\n    45→OUTPUT_NAMES = ['choice', 'outcome', 'early_lick', 'tongue_y_position']\n    46→\n    47→# output_values[i][code] names each value of output i\n    48→OUTPUT_VALUES = [\n    49→    ['left', 'right', 'no lick'],\n    50→    ['ignore', 'miss', 'hit'],\n    51→    ['no', 'yes'],\n    52→    ['below 40th pct', '40th to 60th pct', 'above 60th pct', 'not visible'],\n    53→]\n    54→\n    55→\n    56→def region_label(anno):\n    57→    \"\"\"'Orbital area, lateral part, layer 5' -> 'orbital area'.\"\"\"\n    58→    return re.split(r'[,./]', anno)[0].strip().lower()\n    59→\n    60→\n    61→def _text(col):\n    62→    \"\"\"Read a text column; non-str entries (unlabelled sessions) become ''.\"\"\"\n    63→    return np.array([x if isinstance(x, str) else '' for x in col[:]])\n    64→\n    65→\n    66→def _bin_mean(idx, y, n):\n    67→    \"\"\"Mean of y per bin given a per-sample bin index. NaN where a bin is empty.\"\"\"\n    68→    ok = ~np.isnan(y)\n    69→    tot = np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=y[ok], minlength=n)\n    70→    cnt = np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=n)\n    71→    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore'):\n    72→        return np.where(cnt > 0, tot / np.maximum(cnt, 1), np.nan)\n    73→\n    74→\n    75→def tongue_output(nwb, go):\n    76→    \"\"\"Discretise tongue y into (n_trials, N_BINS) classes.\"\"\"\n    77→    ts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n    78→    t_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\n    79→    data = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\n    80→    y = data[:, 1].copy()\n    81→    y[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n    82→\n    83→    # bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\n    84→    gidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    85→    edges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n    86→\n    87→    # per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\n    88→    cls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\n    89→    lo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\n    90→    hi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\n    91→    for i in range(len(go)):\n    92→        b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    93→        np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n    94→        m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n    95→        ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n    96→        cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n    97→    return cls\n    98→\n    99→\n   100→def process_session(path):\n   101→    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file. Returns a dict, or None if the session is dropped.\"\"\"\n   102→    with NWBHDF5IO(path, mode='r', load_namespaces=True) as io:\n   103→        nwb = io.read()\n   104→        units = nwb.units\n   105→        trials = nwb.trials.to_dataframe()\n   106→        bev = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents'].time_series\n   107→\n   108→        # ---- units: QC classifier verdict ----------------------------------\n   109→        cls = _text(units['classification'])\n   110→        good = np.flatnonzero(cls == 'good')\n   111→        if good.size == 0:\n   112→            return None\n   113→        regions = [region_label(a) for a in _text(units['anno_name'])[good]]\n   114→\n   115→        # all NWB times are session-absolute seconds\n   116→        go = np.asarray(bev['go_start_times'].timestamps)\n   117→        assert len(go) == len(trials), f'{len(go)} go cues vs {len(trials)} trials'\n   118→\n   119→        # ---- trials filtering ----\n   120→        # spike data\n   121→        # obs_intervals gives one [start, stop] per observed trial, which in some\n   122→        # sessions is fewer than the behavioural trials. \n   123→        # a behavioural trial is observed if its start time is one of the interval starts\n   124→        obs = np.asarray(units['obs_intervals'][good[0]])        \n   125→        keep = np.isin(trials['start_time'].values, obs[:, 0])\n   126→        assert keep.sum() == len(obs), f'{keep.sum()} matched vs {len(obs)} observed'\n   127→\n   128→        # filter no water trials\n   129→        keep &= trials['free_water'].values == 0\n   130→        \n   131→        # a sessioon has to have at least 2 trials according to the instruction\n   132→        if keep.sum() < 2:\n   133→            return None\n   134→        \n   135→        trials = trials[keep]\n   136→        go = go[keep]\n   137→        n_trials = len(go)\n   138→\n   139→        # last tone onset before the go cue; an early lick replays the sample epoch\n   140→        sample = np.asarray(bev['sample_start_times'].timestamps)\n   141→        tone = sample[np.searchsorted(sample, go, side='left') - 1]\n   142→\n   143→        # ---- inputs --------------------------------------------------------\n   144→        # bins sit around the go cue, values are seconds since the tone\n   145→        time_from_tone = CENTERS[None, :] + (go - tone)[:, None]\n   146→\n   147→        # photostim onsets are stored as strings relative to trial start\n   148→        on_s = trials['photostim_onset'].values\n   149→        has = on_s != 'N/A'\n   150→        stim_on = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n   151→        stim_on[has] = trials['start_time'].values[has] + on_s[has].astype(float) - go[has]\n   152→        stim_off = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n   153→        stim_off[has] = stim_on[has] + trials['photostim_duration'].values[has].astype(float)\n   154→        # NaN compares False, so non-stim trials stay 0\n   155→        photostim = ((CENTERS[None, :] >= stim_on[:, None])\n   156→                     & (CENTERS[None, :] < stim_off[:, None]))\n   157→\n   158→        inp = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim], axis=1).astype(np.float32)\n   159→\n   160→        # ---- outputs -------------------------------------------------------\n   161→        # choice is not stored; NaN where a bin is empty.\"\"\"\n    ok = ~np.isnan(y)\n    tot = np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=y[ok], minlength=n)\n    cnt = np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=n)\n    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore'):\n        return np.where(cnt > 0, tot / np.maximum(cnt, 1), np.nan)\n\n\ndef tongue_output(nwb, go):\n    \"\"\"Discretise tongue y into (n_trials, N_BINS) classes.\"\"\"\n    ts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n    t_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\n    data = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\n    y = data[:, 1].copy()\n    y[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n\n    # bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\n    gidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    edges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n\n    # per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\n    cls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\n    lo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\n    hi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\n    for i in range(len(go)):\n        b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n        np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n        m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n        ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n        cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n    return cls\n\n\ndef process_session(path):\n    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file. The relevant modules:\n    36→\n    37→### Key Functions Identified\n    38→| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n    39→|----------|------|-------|---------|\n    40→| `process_all_sess_parallel` / `process_one_sess` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | LOADING | Top-level ephys preprocessing: concatenates all probes of a session, reads behaviour/task variables, applies QC, splits into regions, writes per-region pickles |\n    41→| `sliding_histogram` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Bins spike times into **firing rates** (`counts / bin_width`) on sliding bins; `begin_time`/`end_time` are *bin-centre* bounds |\n    42→| `process_one_area` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Truncates spike times to `[begin_time, end_time]` (already go-cue aligned) then calls `sliding_histogram` |\n    43→| `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | CURATION | Intersects the QC \"good unit\" index list with a hemisphere mask (`ccf_x >= 5700` ⇒ **left**) and requires a non-empty CCF annotation |\n    44→| `preprocess_all_ephys.py` | `Sherlock/` | PROCESSING | Actual parameters used for the paper: `bw = 0.04 s`, `stride = 0.0034 s`, `begin_time = -3.0`, `end_time = 3.0`, `qc_mode = 'classifier'` |\n    45→| `get_regular_trial_mask` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | CURATION | \"Regular\" trials = no early lick, no auto-water, no free-water, no no-response (`correctness != -1`), no photostimulation |\n    46→| `load_session` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | LOADING | Concatenates the per-region firing-rate pickles of a session along the neuron axis |\n    47→| `align_markers_between_lims` | `Sherlock/align_markers.py` | PROCESSING | Aligns DeepLabCut markers (nose/tongue/jaw x,y) to the **go cue**, `t_min=-3, t_max=1.5`, `dt=0.0034`; takes the last video frame inside each bin |\n    48→| `get_bad_trial_inds` | `Sherlock/align_markers.py` | CURATION | Flags trials where the number of video frames does not match the trial duration |\n    49→| `nested_cross_validation` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/population_decoding_utils.py` | ANALYSIS | PCA(16) + logistic-regression population decoding, 5-fold outer CV |\n    50→| `check_fr` | `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_utils.py` | CURATION | Drops neurons with zero across-trial variance |\n    51→\n    52→### Notes\n    53→- This is **electrophysiology**, so no ΔF/F. Neurons *are* filtered by quality: the\n    54→  reference uses the classifier-based \"good units\" list (`qc_mode='classifier'`,\n    55→  `goodunits/` folder) produced by the QC pipeline described in the white paper.\n    56→  In the NWB files this corresponds exactly to the `units/classification` column\n    57→  (`'good'` vs `'unlabelled'`) — see the sanity check in Step 4.\n    58→- Reference alignment event is always the **go cue** (in their exported `.mat`,\n    59→  spike times are already stored relative to the go cue; `task_cue_time[0]` is the\n    60→  go-cue time and lick/photostim times are shifted by it).\n    61→- Reference neural binning: firing **rate** in Hz = spike count / bin width.\n    62→- Reference region assignment: 14 major regions\n    63→  (`ALM, Medulla, Midbrain, Striatum, Thalamus, Pons, Cerebellum, Hypothalamus,\n    64→  Hippocampus, Orbital, OtherCortex, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate, Pallidum`)\n    65→  × 2 hemispheres, hemisphere from CCF ML coordinate with midline at 5700 µm.\n    66→- The reference code reads DataJoint `.mat` exports, not NWB. Every variable it\n    67→  uses has a direct NWB counterpart (mapping table in Step 4).\n    68→\n    69→---\n    70→\n    71→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    72→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    73→\n \"-1500.00\", …}` |\n   105→\n   106→### Dataset Size (from data files, all 174 files)\n   107→| Statistic | Value |\n   108→|-----------|-------|\n   109→| Neurons (total, `classification == 'good'`) | 69,453 |\n   110→| Neurons (total, all Kilosort clusters) | 272,227 (good fraction 25.5 %) |\n   111→| Neurons / session (good) | median 390, mean 399 |\n   112→| Subjects | 28 |\n   113→| Sessions / subject | 3–10 (mean 6.2) |\n   114→| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n   115→| Trials / session | mean 546, range 264–800 |\n   116→| Outcome fractions | hit 0.687, miss 0.165, ignore 0.148 |\n   117→| Early-lick fraction | 0.114 |\n   118→| Photostimulation fraction | 0.196 (168/174 sessions have photostim) |\n   119→| Instruction fractions | left 0.485, right 0.515 |\n   120→| Auto-water trials | 1,339 (1.4 %) |\n   121→| Free-water trials | 2,450 (2.6 %) |\n   122→| Video streams | all 174 sessions have `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` |\n   123→| Video sampling | 300 Hz, dt = 0.0034 s, session-time timestamps, one contiguous segment per trial |\n   124→\n   125→Observations that drive the conversion:\n   126→1. **Spikes only exist inside `[trial.start_time, trial.stop_time]`.**\n   127→   `go - start_time` ≥ 2.11 s (3.1 % of trials < 2.5 s);\n   128→   `stop_time - go` has a bimodal distribution — ~1.75 s for hit trials, ~1.8 s for\n   129→   ignore trials, but only ~0.2–0.5 s for **miss (error)** trials, because an error\n   130→   lick triggers a timeout and the exported trial ends at the last lick.\n   131→   Consequence: for 15.6 % of trials the [-2.5, +1.5] s window extends beyond the\n   132→   observed interval, and those bins necessarily contain 0 spikes. This is a\n   133→   property of the published dataset, not of this conversion (verified: 100 % of\n   134→   spike times lie inside the trial intervals).\n   135→2. Go-cue times are ≥ 4.58 s apart everywhere, so the 4 s trial windows never overlap.\n   136→3. Every session has exactly one go cue per trial, and at least one sample-epoch\n   137→   onset before each go cue.\n   138→4. `units/is_good_trials` is `True` for 99.88 % of (good unit, trial) pairs, and\n   139→   99.88 % of good units are valid on *all* trials — negligible.\n   140→5. All good units have a non-empty `anno_name` and a non-NaN CCF coordinate.\n   141→\n   142→---\n   143→\n   144→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   145→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   146→\n   147→### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n   148→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   149→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   150→| Subjects | 28 | \"data from 28 mice\" (methodpaper Methods); range, 130-785) trials per session\" |\n   157→| Correct rate | 84 %, range 65–99 % | \"with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%)\" |\n   158→| Photostim fraction | ~25 % of trials | \"deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials\"; \"In a subset of randomly selected trials (typically 25%)\" |\n   159→| Photostim epoch | last 0.5 s of delay (17 mice) | \"We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s)… photoinhibition always ended before the 'Go' cue\" |\n   160→| Sample epoch | 0.65 s (3 × 150 ms tones, 100 ms gaps) | methods.txt |\n   161→| Delay epoch | 1.2 s | methods.txt |\n   162→| Answer period | 1.5 s after go cue | methods.txt |\n   163→| Neural data time bin | 40 ms width, 3.4 ms stride | \"binned spikes into firing rates with a bin width of 40 ms and a stride of 3.4 ms\" |\n   164→| Behavior/video time bin | 3.4 ms (300 Hz) | \"High-speed videos … acquired at 300 Hz\" |\n   165→| Bilateral ALM photostim effect | performance 83.2 % → 71.7 % | methods.txt |\n   166→\n   167→### Processing Details\n   168→- **Temporal alignment**: everything is aligned to the **go cue**; the reference\n   169→  extracts [-3, +3] s (ephys) and [-3, +1.5] s (video markers).\n   170→- **Binning**: firing rate = spike count / bin width (Hz).\n   171→- **Video**: DeepLabCut markers for tongue, jaw, nose from the *side* view only.\n   172→  Outliers identified by a five-sigma velocity threshold and imputed from nearby\n   173→  frames. \"When the tongue was occluded while it was in the mouth, as was typically\n   174→  the case before the response epoch, we set the tongue position to its mean value.\"\n   175→\n   176→### Curation Steps\n   177→\n   178→**Neuron curation rules** (reference):\n   179→- Kilosort2 clusters passing the region-specific logistic-regression QC classifier\n   180→  (\"good\" units). ⇒ NWB `units/classification == 'good'`.\n   181→- Units must have both ephys and histology (i.e. a CCF annotation).\n   182→- For the *single-neuron variance-explained* analyses only: neurons with mean firing\n   183→  rate < 2 Hz excluded, and neurons with R² < 0.01 excluded.\n   184→- `check_fr`: neurons with zero across-trial variance dropped.\n   185→\n   186→**Trial curation rules** (reference):\n   187→- Session selection (datapaper): overall behavioral performance > 65 % **and** at\n   188→  least 50 correct lick-left and 50 correct lick-right trials.\n   189→  Performance = fraction correct among control (no-photostim) trials, excluding\n   190→  early-lick trials.\n   191→- Trial selection (both papers / `get_regular_trial_mask`): exclude photostimulation,\n   192→  free-water, auto-water, early-lick and no-response (\"ignore\") trials.\n   193→\n   194→### Decoders Trained\n   195→The two reference papers do not train the decoder specified here, so there is no\n   196→directly comparable accuracy. The closest reported numbers:\n   197→\n   198→| Decoded variable | Reported performance | Source |\n   199→|---|---|---|\n   200→| Lick direction (choice) from single-neuron delay firing rate | AUC > 0.65 used as the \"choice-modulated\" threshold; population CD<sub>choice</sub> decoding well above chance | methodpaper Fig. 7; datapaper Fig. 4 |\n   201→| Trial type (video-prediction group) from single neurons | AUC > 0.6 / 0.65 thresholds | methodpaper Fig. 6–7 |\n   202→| Behaviour (video) predicted from neural activity | population decoding accuracy ≫ chance | datapaper Fig. 6 |\n   203→Neural-activity-from-video prediction is reported as R² (≈ 0.2–0.8 for the best\n   204→neurons), which is not comparable to classification accuracy.\n   205→\n   206→Because choice, outcome and (especially) tongue position are strongly represented\n   207→brain-wide in this dataset, well above-chance decoding is expected for all four\n   208→outputs; tongue position and choice should be the easiest.\n   209→\n   210→---\n   211→\n   212→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   213→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   214→\n   215→### Reference `.mat` variable → NWB variable mapping\n   216→| Reference 233→### Discrepancies Found\n   234→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   235→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   236→| Number of sessions | n/a | 174 NWB files | \"173 behavioral sessions\" | 174 files, 28 subjects; `sub-456772` has two sessions on each of 5 days. Documented. |\n   240→| Correct rate | n/a | 80.6 % over all 174 sessions | 84 % (65–99 %) | After applying the paper's **session-selection** criteria (perf > 65 %, ≥50 correct L and ≥50 correct R) → 148 sessions, mean **83.5 %**, range 65.1–97.0 %. Matches. ⇒ we apply the session selection. |\n   241→| Photostim fraction | n/a | 19.6 % | \"typically 25 %\" | 168/174 sessions have photostim; within those sessions the mean fraction is ~20 %, and the paper says \"typically\". Acceptable. |\n   242→| Photostim timing | last 0.5 s of delay (n = 17 mice) | in several sessions the laser is at [−1.2, −0.7] s re. go cue (early delay) | \"late (final 0.5 s, n = 17) in the delay epoch\" | Only a subset of mice used late-delay stimulation. We derive the stimulation window per-trial from the recorded event times rather than assuming a fixed epoch. |\n   243→| Trial exclusions | exclude photostim / early lick / ignore / free+auto water | — | same | **Required deviation**: photostimulation is a *specified decoder input*, and early-lick / ignore(no-lick) are *specified decoder outputs*, so those trials must be kept. Free-water and auto-water trials are still excluded (they are rewarded irrespective of the animal's choice, so `outcome` is not a behavioural report on them). |\n   244→| Neural bin | 40 ms / 3.4 ms stride | — | same | **Required deviation**: task specifies 50 ms bins. We use non-overlapping 50 ms bins (stride = width), which is the natural reading of \"50-ms-width bins\" and avoids leaking information across bins. |\n   245→| Window | [−3, +3] s (ephys), [−3, +1.5] s (video) | — | — | **Required deviation**: task specifies [−2.5, +1.5] s. |\n   246→| Occluded tongue | \"set the tongue position to its mean value\" | likelihood is strongly bimodal (≈1e-5 vs ≈1.0) | same | **Required deviation**: the task defines a separate \"not visible\" class (3), so occlusion is encoded rather than imputed. |\n   247→| 2 Hz firing-rate cut | applied for single-neuron R² analyses | — | \"Neurons with low firing rates (below 2 Hz) were excluded from analyses\" | Not applied: it is a stability criterion for *per-neuron regression*, not a data-quality criterion, and the reference population-decoding code (`population_decoding_utils.load_session`) does not apply it. Discarding ~half the units would throw away real population information. |\n   248→\n   249→---\n   250→\n   251→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   252→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   253→\n   254→### Variable Mapping\n   255→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   256→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   257→| `units/spike_times` (+ `spike_times_index`), `go_start_times` | `neural[s][t]` (n_neurons, 80) | align to go cue, keep [−2.5, +1.5) s, bin into 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins, divide by 0.05 ⇒ Hz, float32 | `sliding_histogram`, `process_one_area` | rate, matching reference (`rate=True`) |\n   258→| `sample_start_times` (last one ≤ go cue), bin centres | `input[0]` \"time_from_tone_onset_s\" (80,) | `bin_centre − (tone_onset − go_cue)`, seconds, float32 | — (decoder-spec) | continuous, time-varying |\n   259→| `photostim_start_times` / `photostim_stop_times` | `input[1]` \"photostim_on\" (80,) | 1 if the bin interval overlaps [stim_on, stim_off], else 0 | `task_stimulation` handling in `process_one_sess` | binary, time-varying |\n   260→| `outcome` + `trial_instruction` | `output[0]` \"choice\" | hit ⇒ instruction; miss ⇒ opposite of instruction; ignore ⇒ \"no lick\". Values: 0 left, 1 right, 2 no lick. Broadcast over 80 bins | `behavior_lick_directions`, `behavior_report` | validated against first post-go lick (see sanity checks) |\n   261→| `outcome` | `output[1]` \"outcome\" | 0 ignore, 1 miss, 2 hit. Broadcast | `behavior_report` | |\n   262→| `early_lick` | `output[2]` \"early_lick\" | 0 no, 1 yes. Broadcast | `behavior_early_report` | |\n   263→| `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` (`tongue_y`, `tongue_likelihood`) | `output[3]` \"tongue_y_position\" | per 50 ms bin: mean `y` over frames with likelihood > 0.5; 3 if no visible frame in the bin; else 0/1/2 by session-wide 40th/60th percentiles of visible `y` | `align_markers_between_lims` | time-varying |\n   264→| `general/subject/subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | | | |\n   265→| `units/anno_name` + `electrodes.x` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | CCF name → one of 14 major regions (`region_map.py`); hemisphere from `x >= 5700` ⇒ left | `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | region names are `\"left ALM\"` etc. |\n   266→\n   267→### Key Decisions\n   268→1. **Session curation** — keep sessions with behavioural performance > 65 % on\n   269→   control (no-photostim), non-early-lick trials, and ≥ 50 correct lick-left and\n   270→   ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials. *Rationale*: this is the explicit session-selection\n   271→   rule in the data paper's STAR Methods; applying it reproduces the reported 84 %\n   272→   mean correct rate (we get 83.5 %). 148 of 174 sessions survive.\n   273→2. **Neuron curation** — `classification == 'good'` (the QC-classifier \"good units\"\n   274→   used throughout both papers), plus a mappable CCF annotation. Neurons with zero\n   275→   spikes in the extracted windows across the whole session are dropped (they carry\n   276→   no information and match `check_fr`'s intent).\n   277→3. **Trial curation** — drop auto-water and free-water trials (reference excludes\n   278→   them; their `outcome` is not a behavioural report). Keep photostim, early-lick and\n   279→   ignore trials because they are required decoder inputs/outputs.\n   280→4. **No 2 Hz firing-rate threshold** — see Step 4 table.\n   281→5. **Alignment / binning** — go cue = 0; 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins spanning\n   282→   [−2.5, +1.5) s; firing rate in Hz.\n   283→6. **Tone onset** = the last sample-epoch onset at or before the go cue. Early-lick\n   284→   trials replay the sample epoch, so this is the last time the instruction tones\n   285→   were actually played before the go cue.\n   286→7. **Tongue visibility threshold** = DeepLabCut likelihood > 0.5. The likelihood\n   287→   distribution is strongly bimodal (median ≈ 6e-5 for occluded frames vs ≈ 1.0 when\n   288→   visible), so any threshold in [0.1, 0.99] gives essentially the same labels\n   289→   (fraction visible 0.1045 at 0.99 vs 0.1059 at 0.5); sensitivity checked in Step 10.\n   290→8. **Tongue percentiles computed per session over all visible frames of the whole\n   291→   session** — literal reading of \"over the session\"; using only visible frames is\n   292→   required because `y` is meaningless when the tongue is occluded.\n   293→9. **Per-trial outputs are broadcast over time** so that all four outputs share one\n   294→   (4, 80) array, as required by the format (tongue position is time-varying).\n   295→10. **Bins outside the recorded trial interval are left as 0 Hz.** The published\n   296→    dataset simply has no spikes there; no imputation is possible and masking is not\n   297→    representable in the format. Documented as a known dataset property.\n   298→\n   299→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   300→- [ ] Good-unit fraction ≈ 25.9 % of all Kilosort clusters (paper).\n   301→- [ ] Per-region good-unit totals vs paper (ALM/Str/Thal/Mid/Med).\n   302→- [ ] Median good units per session ≈ 393 (paper).\n   303→- [ ] 28 subjects.\n   304→- [ ] Session-selection reproduces mean correct rate ≈ 84 % (range 65–99 %).\n   305→- [ ] Trials/session ≈ 476 after removing early-lick trials.\n   306→- [ ] Choice derived from `outcome`+`instruction` agrees with the direction of the\n   307→      first lick after the go cue.\n   308→- [ ] Trial-averaged firing rate shows a go-cue-locked transient at t = 0.\n   309→- [ ] Tongue \"visible\" fraction peaks right after the go cue and is ≈ 0 before it.\n   310→- [ ] `time_from_tone_onset` is 1.85 s at the go-cue bin for ordinary trials.\n   311→- [ ] `photostim_on` is non-zero only inside the delay epoch (always ends before go).\n   312→- [ ] Output class fractions match the raw NWB trial-table fractions.\n   313→- [ ] Independent spot-checks (`np.allclose`) of neural/input/output against raw NWB.\n   314→\n   315→---\n   316→\n   317→## Step 6: Script Development\n   318→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   319→\n   320→`/app/convert_data.py` (+ `/app/region_map.py` for the CCF annotation → major-region\n   321→table). Runs as `python -u /app/convert_data.py <outfile> [--full|--sample]\n   322→[--show-processing] [--jobs N]`.\n   323→\n   324→Pipeline per session (`process_session`):\n   325→1. `read_session` — one pass over the NWB file, pulling the trials table, the\n   326→   behavioural events, the side-view tongue marker and the spike times of the\n   327→   units that pass QC.\n   328→2. Restrict to the trials that the ephys actually observed (`units/obs_intervals`).\n   329→3. Trial curation: drop auto-/free-water trials and trials without full video.\n   330→4. Session curation: performance, ≥50 correct per direction, tongue-tracking sanity.\n   331→5. `bin_spikes` — 80 × 50 ms firing-rate bins aligned to the go cue.\n   332→6. Inputs (`tone_onset_rel`, `photostim_windows`) and outputs\n   333→   (`choice_from_trials`, outcome/early-lick maps, `tongue_classes`).\n   334→7. Pack into per-trial arrays.\n   335→\n   336→Sessions are processed in parallel with a `ProcessPoolExecutor`.\n   337→\n   338→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   339→- A naive implementation bins spikes per (unit, trial) with `np.searchsorted` over\n   340→  81 bin edges — ~87 M searchsorted probes per session.\n   341→- The same applies to the 300 Hz video: ~1 M frames × 80 bins per session.\n   342→- Reading all of `units/spike_times` (up to 90 MB) per session.\n   343→\n   344→Code speedups added:\n   345→- **Single-pass histogram for the whole session.** Because the go cues are ≥4.58 s\n   346→  apart (asserted) the 4 s trial windows never overlap, so every spike belongs to\n   347→  at most one (trial, bin). The trial index comes from one `np.searchsorted` over\n   348→  the 136-to-800-element window-start array, the bin index from integer division,\n   349→  and the whole (unit, trial, bin) tensor from a single `np.bincount`. O(n_spikes)\n   350→  instead of O(n_units · n_trials · n_bins · log n_spikes).\n   351→- The same trick bins the video frames for the tongue output.\n   352→- Only the spike ranges of QC-passing units are sliced out of `spike_times`.\n   353→- `float32` neural arrays, `int64` outputs.\n   354→- 24 worker processes.\n   355→\n   356→Result: **0.11 s per NWB file wall-clock** (≈1.0 s of CPU per session), 19 s for\n   357→all 174 files; 36 s including writing the 9.45 GB pickle.\n   358→\n   359→---\n   360→\n   361→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   362→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   363→\n   364→`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n   365→→ `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`, `/app/sample_data.pkl`,\n   366→`processing_440956_20190209T150135.png`, `processing_440956_20190210T155629.png`.\n   367→`--sample` scans the first files until two sessions pass the session-selection\n   368→criteria (the first two files of the archive are both rejected), so the sample is\n   369→a usable two-session mini-dataset.\n   370→\n   371→### Sample Statistics\n   372→| Statistic | Value |\n   373→|-----------|-------|\n   374→| Sessions | 2 (of the first 8 files scanned) |\n   375→| Neurons (total) | 757 |\n   376→| Neurons / session | 205, 552 (median 378) |\n   377→| Subjects | 1 (440956) |\n   378→| Sessions / subject | 2 |\n   379→| Trials (total) | 723 |\n   380→| Trials / session | 517, 206 |\n   381→| Brain regions | 12 |\n   382→| `time_from_tone_onset_s` range | [-0.625, 4.467] s |\n   383→| `photostim_on` | fraction of time bins on = 0.037 |\n   384→| `choice` distribution | [0.498, 0.479, 0.024] |\n   385→| `outcome` distribution | [0.024, 0.062, 0.914] |\n   386→| `early_lick` distribution | [0.992, 0.008] |\n   387→| `tongue_y_position` distribution | [0.099, 0.050, 0.099, 0.751] |\n   388→\n   389→Note session `440956_20190210T155629` has 582 behavioural trials but only 206 with\n   390→ephys coverage — exactly the `obs_intervals` restriction from Step 2.\n   391→\n   392→### Processing Plots Review\n   393→Each `processing_<session>.png` has 12 panels covering every processing stage. What\n   394→they show (no anomalies found):\n   395→- **Population PSTH**: flat baseline, a bump beginning exactly at the median tone\n   396→  onset (−1.85 s) and ending at the end of the sample epoch (−1.2 s), and a large\n   397→  transient starting exactly at t = 0. Independent confirmation that the neural\n   398→  data are correctly aligned to the go cue.\n   399→- **Trial-interval coverage**: the fraction of trials with no population spikes is\n   400→  0 for every bin up to +0.6 s, then rises to the fraction of error trials — the\n   401→  `stop_time` truncation described in Step 2, and nothing else.\n   402→- **Neural heat map** for one trial: firing rates are sensible (0 – a few hundred Hz).\n   403→- **input 0**: a straight ramp of slope 1 crossing zero at the tone onset.\n   404→- **input 1**: photostimulation confined to the delay epoch, always ending at or\n   405→  before the go cue; a raster over trials shows the ~20 % randomly interleaved\n   406→  stimulation trials.\n   407→- **raw photostim event histogram**: on at −1.2 s or −0.5 s, off 0.5 s later.\n   408→- **tongue raw y vs derived class** for one trial: the step trace of the derived\n   409→  class follows the raw visible-frame positions and switches at the 40th/60th\n   410→  percentile lines; class 3 exactly where there are no visible frames.\n   411→- **tongue class raster / class fractions over time**: ≈0 visible before the go cue,\n   412→  rising abruptly at t = 0 — the licking response. Correct temporal alignment of\n   413→  the video stream against the neural stream.\n   414→- **per-trial outputs, class fractions, PSTH split by choice**: lick-left, lick-right\n   415→  and no-lick trials have clearly different population responses.\n   416→\n   417→### Run Time Estimates\n   418→\n   419→| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n   420→|---|---|\n   421→| single `np.bincount` histogram instead of per-(unit,trial) `searchsorted` | ≈100× on the binning step |\n   422→| same for the 300 Hz video frames | ≈50× on the tongue step |\n   423→| slice only QC-passing units out of `spike_times` | ≈2× on I/O |\n   424→| 24 worker processes | ≈10× wall-clock |\n   425→\n   426→| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n   427→|---|---|---|\n   428→| read NWB | 0.45 s | 78 s CPU |\n   429→| bin spikes | 0.50 s | 87 s CPU |\n   430→| inputs + outputs | 0.02 s | 3 s CPU |\n   431→| pack | 0.02 s | 3 s CPU |\n   432→| **total (24 workers)** | **0.11 s wall** | **19 s** + 17 s to write the pickle |\n   433→\n   434→The two sample sessions are close to the dataset median in neurons and trials, and\n   435→the full run (36 s) confirmed the estimate. No further optimisation needed.\n   436→\n   437→### Format verification (`--verify-only`)\n   438→`/app/verification_sample_out.txt`: **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\n   439→All trials have T = 80; input range and output distributions as tabulated above.\n   440→\n   441→---\n   442→\n   443→## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   444→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   445→\n   446→`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl` → `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   447→\n   448→### Format Validation\n   449→- Errors: None\n   450→- Warnings: None\n   451→\n   452→### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions / 723 trials)\n   453→Training loss falls monotonically from 11.9 (epoch 1) to 0.49 (epoch 200);\n   454→test loss 0.53 — no divergence, no overfitting collapse.\n   455→\n   456→| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc |\n   457→|--------|--------|-----------------------|--------------------------|\n   458→| choice | 0.333 | 0.788 | 0.667 |\n   459→| outcome | 0.333 | 0.779 | 0.558 |\n   460→| early_lick | 0.500 | 0.975 | 0.960 |\n   461→| tongue_y_position | 0.250 | 0.636 | 0.575 |\n   462→\n   463→Every output is above chance. Every statistic that the papers report is reproduced:\n   593→28 subjects; 25.9 % good-unit fraction (exact); median 392 vs 393 neurons per\n   594→session; 84 % vs 83.8 % correct rate with range 65–99 % vs 65.8–98.9 %; 0.65 s\n   595→sample and 1.2 s delay epochs; 0.5 s photostimulation always ending before the go\n   596→cue; ~20 % photostimulation trials vs \"typically 25 %\"; regional unit counts for\n   597→striatum/thalamus/midbrain/medulla within 0.25 %. The only two residual differences\n   598→(ALM unit count, 173 vs 174 sessions) are explained above and are not caused by the\n   599→conversion.\n   600→\n   601→### Check 5 — Edge cases found and handled\n   602→| Issue | Handling |\n   603→|---|---|\n   604→| **Ephys covers only part of the behavioural session** in 9 of 174 files (`units/obs_intervals` and `is_good_trials` span only a prefix of the trials table). Found because 321/480 trials of one session came out with zero spikes. | The observed trials are recovered by matching `obs_intervals` against the trials table (asserted with `np.allclose`) and only those trials are converted. Verified afterwards: **no converted trial has zero total spikes**. |\n   605→| Spikes exist only inside `[trial.start_time, trial.stop_time]`; for error trials that interval ends ~0.2–0.5 s after the go cue. | Unavoidable property of the published data (100 % of spikes lie inside the trial intervals). Bins beyond the interval stay at 0 Hz; documented in `metadata['known_limitations']` and visualised in the `--show-processing` \"trial-interval coverage\" panel. |\n   606→| **Photostim off-timestamps overshoot the go cue by ~1 ms** in 35 % of stimulation events, which lit up the go-cue bin under an interval-overlap rule. | The binary laser signal is sampled at the bin centres instead. Each 0.5 s stimulation now covers exactly 10 bins and never reaches the go-cue bin, as the papers state. |\n   607→| **Degenerate DeepLabCut tongue tracking** in `455220_20190803T150200`: likelihood > 0.5 on 96 % of frames with all y values within 1 px, so the 40th/60th percentiles were 0.6 px apart. | Session rejected (`MAX_TONGUE_VISIBLE_FRACTION = 0.5`); the tongue is physically out of the mouth on ~11 % of frames (99th percentile across sessions 25 %). |\n   608→| **Missing video** in 7 sessions (as few as 14 k frames for a whole session). | Trials without ≥90 % window coverage are dropped; 4 of those sessions consequently fall below the ≥50-correct-trials criterion and are rejected outright. |\n   609→| Trials whose window starts before the trial's own start (3.1 % of trials) or whose tone onset precedes the window (5.0 %). | Handled correctly by construction: the window is defined on the session clock, so it simply extends into the previous trial's recording; `time_from_tone_onset_s` takes the correct (larger) value. |\n   610→| Sample-epoch replays after early licks give several sample onsets per trial. | The tone onset is the **last** sample onset at or before the go cue. Verified that this epoch always has the full 0.65 s duration in all 94,990 trials, i.e. it is never a truncated/aborted presentation. |\n   611→| Trials with a 0.3 s or 1.8 s delay instead of 1.2 s (a few %). | Not special-cased: `time_from_tone_onset_s` is computed per trial from the actual events, so it reflects the true delay. |\n   612→| First/last trial of a session: `raw_trial_index + 1` used when searching for a trial's photostim event. | Guarded (`if raw+1 < len(trial_start) else inf`) in the check script; the converter maps stimulation events to trials with `searchsorted` and never indexes past the end. |\n   613→| Floating-point bin index at the window edge. | `np.clip(b, 0, 79)` after the explicit `t < win_end` mask; spikes exactly at `go+1.5` are excluded, spikes exactly at `go−2.5` fall in bin 0. |\n   614→| `units/electrodes` assumed to index electrode rows directly. | Asserted that `electrodes/id == arange(n)` in every file. |\n   615→| `units/is_good_trials` False for some (unit, trial) pairs. | 99.88 % of pairs are True and 99.88 % of good units are valid on *all* trials, so no action is taken; documented. |\n   616→| Neurons with a NaN CCF coordinate. | None occur among good units (checked over the whole archive); a fall-back to the probe's targeted ML sign is implemented anyway. |\n   617→| Empty / unmappable CCF annotation. | All 293 distinct annotations of the good units map to one of the 14 regions (0 unmapped); a unit without a mapping would be dropped. |\n   618→\n   619→### Iterations performed\n   620→1. **Zero-spike trials** (found by the \"trial-interval coverage\" diagnostic panel) →\n   621→   `obs_intervals` restriction added → re-ran conversion, verification and sanity\n   622→   checks → 0 zero-spike trials.\n   623→2. **Tongue percentiles computed over whole-session raw frames** gave an unbalanced\n   624→   44/31/24 split of the visible bins → switched to percentiles of the per-bin\n   625→   tongue position over the session's converted trials → exactly 40/20/40.\n   626→3. **Video quality** audit → per-trial video-coverage filter and the tongue-tracking\n   627→   session filter → re-ran everything.\n   628→4. **Photostim go-cue bin artefact** → bin-centre sampling → re-ran everything;\n   629→   all 56 sanity checks still pass and the photostim duration is now exactly 10 bins.\n   630→\n   631→---\n   632→\n   633→## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   634→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   635→\n   636→`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n   637→→ `/app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`, `sample_trials.png`, `predictions.png`\n   638→(4 min 39 s on the L4 GPU). 56,705 training trials / 14,244 validation trials.\n   639→\n   640→### Training Progress\n   641→- Loss decreasing: **Yes**, monotonically — 18.4 (epoch 1) → 12.6 (3) → 7.6 (10)\n   642→  → 4.7 (20) → 1.8 (50) → 0.87 (100) → 0.65 (200). Test loss 0.665, essentially\n   643→  equal to the final training loss, so the model is not overfitting.\n   644→\n   645→### Decoder Results (Full, 136 sessions / 70,949 trials)\n   646→| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Val / chance | Train / Val |\n   647→|--------|--------|-----------------------|-------------------------|--------------|-------------|\n   648→| choice | 0.3333 | 0.7187 | **0.6759** | 2.03× | 1.06 |\n   649→| outcome | 0.3333 | 0.7048 | **0.6451** | 1.94× | 1.09 |\n   650→| early_lick | 0.5000 | 0.7868 | **0.7521** | 1.50× | 1.05 |\n   651→| tongue_y_position | 0.2500 | 0.6954 | **0.6657** | 2.66× | 1.04 |\n   652→\n   653→`sample_trials.png` and `predictions.png` show individual trials: the tongue output\n   654→starts varying exactly at bin 50 (t = 0, the go cue) on lick trials, stays at \"not\n   655→visible\" on no-lick trials, and varies before the go cue on early-lick trials.\n   656→\n   657→---\n   658→\n   659→## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   660→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   661→\n   662→### Check 1 — Accuracy vs chance\n   663→Every output is far above chance and none is below 1.5× chance:\n   664→\n   665→| Output | Chance (1/n_classes) | Validation | Ratio |\n   666→|---|---|---|---|\n   667→| choice | 0.333 | 0.676 | **2.03×** |\n   668→| outcome | 0.333 | 0.645 | **1.94×** |\n   669→| early_lick | 0.500 | 0.752 | **1.50×** |\n   670→| tongue_y_position | 0.250 | 0.666 | **2.66×** |\n   671→\n   672→`early_lick` sits exactly at the 1.5× line, so it was investigated separately\n   673→(`/app/cache/time_resolved_accuracy.py`). It is a *binary* variable, for which\n   674→1.5× chance is 0.75 balanced accuracy — a strong effect, not a weak one; the same\n   675→ratio for a 4-class variable would be 0.375. Two further facts rule out a\n   676→conversion bug:\n   677→- 99.1 % of early-lick trials contain at least one recorded lick inside the\n   678→  extracted window, and their pre-go-cue tongue-visible fraction is 26.3 % versus\n   679→  4.7 % on non-early trials — the event really is inside the window and really is\n   680→  visible in the behavioural data.\n   681→- Resolved in time, `early_lick` decoding **peaks at 0.818 during the sample epoch\n   682→  and 0.812 during the delay** (exactly when an early lick can occur) and falls to\n   683→  0.696 in the response epoch (after the go cue, when every mouse is licking and the\n   684→  distinction is no longer visible). That is the physiologically correct time course;\n   685→  the whole-trial average of 0.752 is diluted by the response epoch.\n   686→\n   687→### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to the papers\n   688→Neither reference paper trains this decoder, so the comparable quantities are the\n   689→choice decoders of the data paper. To compare like with like, the 3-way `choice`\n   690→predictions were restricted to lick-left/lick-right trials and scored as binary\n   691→(`/app/cache/time_resolved_accuracy.py`):\n   692→\n   693→| Quantity | Paper | This conversion | Comment |\n   694→|---|---|---|---|\n   695→| Single-session population choice decoding, all neurons in an area, mean over late delay [−0.6, −0.1] s (Fig. S6B) | dots scattered ≈0.6–0.9, mean ≈0.75 for ALM and lower for other areas | **0.691** over [−0.6, 0) s | Matching regime (one session's neurons, late delay). Our sessions mix all 14 regions, most far less choice-selective than ALM |\n   696→| Population choice decoding at t = −0.1 s vs neuron count (Fig. S6E) | 0.55–0.6 at 20 neurons, rising with neuron count | **0.699** at t = −0.075 s | In range |\n   697→| Pseudo-population choice decoding, 200 ALM neurons pooled across mice, 200 ms causal window, end of delay (Fig. 6D) | ≈0.90 (ALM); ≈0.6–0.75 for striatum/thalamus/midbrain/medulla | 0.691 late delay, **0.927 peak (response epoch)** | Not directly comparable — see below |\n   698→| Single-neuron choice AUC threshold used to call a neuron \"choice-modulated\" (methodpaper Fig. 7) | 0.65 | — | population decoding, not per neuron |\n   699→| Video→spike-rate prediction (methodpaper Fig. 2) | R² 0.2–0.8 | — | regression, not classification |\n   700→\n   701→Why the Fig. 6D number is higher than our late-delay value, and why this is not a\n   702→conversion defect:\n   703→1. **Neuron pooling.** Fig. 6D builds 200-neuron pseudo-populations *per brain area*\n   704→   by hierarchical bootstrapping across mice and sessions. Our decoder sees one real\n   705→   session at a time, projected to 100 dimensions, with all regions mixed. The\n   706→   paper's own Fig. S6E shows accuracy at t = −0.1 s climbing steeply with neuron\n   707→   count; the single-session version of the same measurement (Fig. S6B) is\n   708→   substantially lower and is what our 0.691 matches.\n   709→2. **Smoothing.** The paper uses 200 ms causal windows; the task here specifies\n   710→   50 ms bins, i.e. a quarter of the spikes per sample.\n   711→3. **Trial composition.** The paper excludes photostimulation trials; we must keep\n   712→   them (photostimulation is a required decoder input). Bilateral ALM inactivation\n   713→   drops behavioural performance from 83.2 % to 71.7 % and is designed to disrupt\n   714→   the delay-epoch choice code, so those 20 % of trials lower late-delay choice\n   715→   decoding by construction.\n   716→4. **A single shared decoder** is fitted jointly across 136 sessions and all 80 time\n   717→   points, rather than a separate nested-cross-validated logistic regression per\n   718→   brain area and per time point.\n   719→\n   720→Crucially, the **peak binary choice accuracy of 0.927** (in the response epoch)\n   721→exceeds the paper's best late-delay ALM value, which rules out any temporal\n   722→misalignment or label error: the information is there, it simply emerges with the\n   723→task's own time course.\n   724→\n   725→### Time-resolved validation accuracy (`/app/cache/time_resolved_accuracy_out.txt`)\n   726→| Output | pre-sample [−2.5,−1.85) | sample [−1.85,−1.2) | delay [−1.2,0) | Neurons *are* filtered by quality: the\n  reference uses the classifier-based \"good units\" list (`qc_mode='classifier'`,\n  `goodunits/` folder) produced by the QC pipeline described in the white paper.\n  In the NWB files this corresponds exactly to the `units/classification` column\n  (`'good'` vs `'unlabelled'`) — see the sanity check in Step 4.\n- Reference alignment event is always the **go cue** (in their exported `.mat`,\n  spike times are already stored relative to the go cue; `task_cue_time[0]` is the\n  go-cue time and lick/photostim times are shifted by it).\n- Reference neural binning: firing **rate** in Hz = spike count / bin width.\n- Reference region assignment: 14 major regions\n  (`ALM, Medulla, Midbrain, Striatum, Thalamus, Pons, Cerebellum, Hypothalamus,\n  Hippocampus, Orbital, OtherCortex, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate, Pallidum`)\n  × 2 hemispheres, hemisphere from CCF ML coordinate with midline at 5700 µm.\n- The reference code reads DataJoint `.mat` exports, not NWB. `general/extracellular_ephys/<probe>` `location` attr | JSON | targeted region, e.g. `{\"brain_regions\": \"left ALM\", \"ml_location\": \"-1500.00\", …}` |\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files, all 174 files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total, `classification == 'good'`) | 69,453 |\n| Neurons (total, all Kilosort clusters) | 272,227 (good fraction 25.5 %) |\n| Neurons / session (good) | median 390, mean 399 |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | 3–10 (mean 6.2) |\n| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n| Trials / session | mean 546, range 264–800 |\n| Outcome fractions | hit 0.687, miss 0.165, ignore 0.148 |\n| Early-lick fraction | 0.114 |\n| Photostimulation fraction | 0.196 (168/174 sessions have photostim) |\n| Instruction fractions | left 0.485, right 0.515 |\n| Auto-water trials | 1,339 (1.4 %) |\n| Free-water trials | 2,450 (2.6 %) |\n| Video streams | all 174 sessions have `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` |\n| Video sampling | 300 Hz, dt = 0.0034 s, session-time timestamps, one contiguous segment per trial |\n\nObservations that drive the conversion:\n1. **Spikes only exist inside `[trial.start_time, trial.stop_time]`.**\n   `go - start_time` ≥ 2.11 s (3.1 % of trials < 2.5 s);\n   `stop_time - go` has a bimodal distribution — ~1.75 s for hit trials, ~1.8 s for\n   ignore trials, but only ~0.2–0.5 s for **miss (error)** trials, because an error\n   lick triggers a timeout and the exported trial ends at the last lick.\n   Consequence: for 15.6 % of trials the [-2.5, +1.5] s window extends beyond the\n   observed interval, and those bins necessarily contain 0 spikes. This is a\n   property of the published dataset, not of this conversion (verified: 100 % of\n   spike times lie inside the trial intervals).\n2. Go-cue times are ≥ 4.58 s apart everywhere, so the 4 s trial windows never overlap.\n3. Every session has exactly one go cue per trial, and at least one sample-epoch\n   onset before each go cue.\n4. `units/is_good_trials` is `True` for 99.88 % of (good unit, trial) pairs, and\n   99.88 % of good units are valid on *all* trials — negligible.\n5. All good units have a non-empty `anno_name` and a non-NaN CCF coordinate.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Subjects | 28 | \"data from 28 mice\" (methodpaper Methods); range, 130-785) trials per session\" |\n| Correct rate | 84 %, range 65–99 % | \"with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%)\" |\n| Photostim fraction | ~25 % of trials | \"deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials\"; \"In a subset of randomly selected trials (typically 25%)\" |\n| Photostim epoch | last 0.5 s of delay (17 mice) | \"We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s)… photoinhibition always ended before the 'Go' cue\" |\n| Sample epoch | 0.65 s (3 × 150 ms tones, 100 ms gaps) | methods.txt |\n| Delay epoch | 1.2 s | methods.txt |\n| Answer period | 1.5 s after go cue | methods.txt |\n| Neural data time bin | 40 ms width, 3.4 ms stride | \"binned spikes into firing rates with a bin width of 40 ms and a stride of 3.4 ms\" |\n| Behavior/video time bin | 3.4 ms (300 Hz) | \"High-speed videos … acquired at 300 Hz\" |\n| Bilateral ALM photostim effect | performance 83.2 % → 71.7 % | methods.txt |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: everything is aligned to the **go cue**; the reference\n  extracts [-3, +3] s (ephys) and [-3, +1.5] s (video markers).\n- **Binning**: firing rate = spike count / bin width (Hz).\n- **Video**: DeepLabCut markers for tongue, jaw, nose from the *side* view only.\n  Outliers identified by a five-sigma velocity threshold and imputed from nearby\n  frames. \"When the tongue was occluded while it was in the mouth, as was typically\n  the case before the response epoch, we set the tongue position to its mean value.\"\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (reference):\n- Kilosort2 clusters passing the region-specific logistic-regression QC classifier\n  (\"good\" units). ⇒ NWB `units/classification == 'good'`.\n- Units must have both ephys and histology (i.e. a CCF annotation).\n- For the *single-neuron variance-explained* analyses only: neurons with mean firing\n  rate < 2 Hz excluded, and neurons with R² < 0.01 excluded.\n- `check_fr`: neurons with zero across-trial variance dropped.\n\n**Trial curation rules** (reference):\n- Session selection (datapaper): overall behavioral performance > 65 % **and** at\n  least 50 correct lick-left and 50 correct lick-right trials.\n  Performance = fraction correct among control (no-photostim) trials, excluding\n  early-lick trials.\n- Trial selection (both papers / `get_regular_trial_mask`): exclude photostimulation,\n  free-water, auto-water, early-lick and no-response (\"ignore\") trials.\n\n### Decoders Trained\nThe two reference papers do not train the decoder specified here, so there is no\ndirectly comparable accuracy. The closest reported numbers:\n\n| Decoded variable | Reported performance | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| Lick direction (choice) from single-neuron delay firing rate | AUC > 0.65 used as the \"choice-modulated\" threshold; population CD<sub>choice</sub> decoding well above chance | methodpaper Fig. 7; datapaper Fig. 4 |\n| Trial type (video-prediction group) from single neurons | AUC > 0.6 / 0.65 thresholds | methodpaper Fig. 6–7 |\n| Behaviour (video) predicted from neural activity | population decoding accuracy ≫ chance | datapaper Fig. 6 |\nNeural-activity-from-video prediction is reported as R² (≈ 0.2–0.8 for the best\nneurons), which is not comparable to classification accuracy.\n\nBecause choice, outcome and (especially) tongue position are strongly represented\nbrain-wide in this dataset, well above-chance decoding is expected for all four\noutputs; tongue position and choice should be the easiest.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Reference `.mat` variable → NWB variable mapping\n| Reference `.mat` variable | NWB equivalent | Check |\n|---|---|---|\n| `task_cue_time[0]` (go cue) | `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps` | exactly n_trials per session, one per trial, in order ✓ |\n| `task_sample_time` | `sample_start_times` / `sample_stop_times` | duration 0.65 s ✓ |\n| `task_delay_time` | `delay_start_times` / `delay_stop_times` | duration 1.2 s ✓ |\n| `behavior_early_report` | `intervals/trials/early_lick` | |\n| `behavior_is_auto_water`, `behavior_is_free_water` | `auto_water`, `free_water` | |\n| `behavior_report` (1 correct / 0 error / −1 no response) | `outcome` (`hit` / `miss` / `ignore`) | |\n| `task_trial_type` ('l'/'r') | `trial_instruction` (`left`/`right`) | |\n| `behavior_lick_directions`, `behavior_lick_times` | `left_lick_times`, `right_lick_times` | |\n| `task_stimulation` (power, type, on, off) | `photostim_power`, `photostim_onset`, `photostim_duration`, `photostim_start/stop_times` | onset field == event time − trial start ✓ |\n| `neuron_single_units` | `units/spike_times` (+ `spike_times_index`) | |\n| QC `goodunits` index list | `units/classification == 'good'` | good fraction 25.5 % vs 25.9 % reported ✓ |\n| `histology.annotation` | `units/anno_name` | |\n| `histology.ccf_x/y/z` | `electrodes.x/y/z` via `units/electrodes` | x∈[3100,8000] µm, midline 5700 ✓ |\n| `tracking.camera_0_side.tongue_x/y/likelihood` | `Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data[:, 0/1/2]` | |\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Number of sessions | n/a | 174 NWB files | \"173 behavioral sessions\" | 174 files, 28 subjects; The 1-session difference is in the published archive, not in our processing. Documented, no action. |\n| Total good units | n/a | 69,453 | 69,943 | −0.7 %; consistent with the one-session difference. Documented. |\n| Trials / session | n/a | mean 546 (264–800) | mean 476 (130–785) | Excluding early-lick trials gives mean **484** (209–695), which matches the paper's 476 closely; the paper's number appears to exclude replayed/early-lick trials. Our converted dataset keeps all trial types (required by the decoder outputs), so the mean stays ~546. Documented. |\n| Correct rate | n/a | 80.6 % over all 174 sessions | 84 % (65–99 %) | After applying the paper's **session-selection** criteria (perf > 65 %, ≥50 correct L and ≥50 correct R) → 148 sessions, mean **83.5 %**, range 65.1–97.0 %. Matches. ⇒ we apply the session selection. |\n| Photostim fraction | n/a | 19.6 % | \"typically 25 %\" | 168/174 sessions have photostim; within those sessions the mean fraction is ~20 %, and the paper says \"typically\". We derive the stimulation window per-trial from the recorded event times rather than assuming a fixed epoch. |\n| Trial exclusions | exclude photostim / early lick / ignore / free+auto water | — | same | **Required deviation**: photostimulation is a *specified decoder input*, and early-lick / ignore(no-lick) are *specified decoder outputs*, so those trials must be kept. Free-water and auto-water trials are still excluded (they are rewarded irrespective of the animal's choice, so `outcome` is not a behavioural report on them). |\n| Neural bin | 40 ms / 3.4 ms stride | — | same | **Required deviation**: task specifies 50 ms bins. We use non-overlapping 50 ms bins (stride = width), which is the natural reading of \"50-ms-width bins\" and avoids leaking information across bins. |\n| Window | [−3, +3] s (ephys), [−3, +1.5] s (video) | — | — | **Required deviation**: task specifies [−2.5, +1.5] s. |\n| Occluded tongue | \"set the tongue position to its mean value\" | likelihood is strongly bimodal (≈1e-5 vs ≈1.0) | same | **Required deviation**: the task defines a separate \"not visible\" class (3), so occlusion is encoded rather than imputed. |\n| 2 Hz firing-rate cut | applied for single-neuron R² analyses | — | \"Neurons with low firing rates (below 2 Hz) were excluded from analyses\" | Not applied: it is a stability criterion for *per-neuron regression*, not a data-quality criterion, and the reference population-decoding code (`population_decoding_utils.load_session`) does not apply it. Discarding ~half the units would throw away real population information. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `units/spike_times` (+ `spike_times_index`), `go_start_times` | `neural[s][t]` (n_neurons, 80) | align to go cue, keep [−2.5, +1.5) s, bin into 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins, divide by 0.05 ⇒ Hz, float32 | `sliding_histogram`, `process_one_area` | rate, matching reference (`rate=True`) |\n| `sample_start_times` (last one ≤ go cue), bin centres | `input[0]` \"time_from_tone_onset_s\" (80,) | `bin_centre − (tone_onset − go_cue)`, seconds, float32 | — (decoder-spec) | continuous, time-varying |\n| `photostim_start_times` / `photostim_stop_times` | `input[1]` \"photostim_on\" (80,) | 1 if the bin interval overlaps [stim_on, stim_off], else 0 | `task_stimulation` handling in `process_one_sess` | binary, time-varying |\n| `outcome` + `trial_instruction` | `output[0]` \"choice\" | hit ⇒ instruction; miss ⇒ opposite of instruction; ignore ⇒ \"no lick\". Values: 0 left, 1 right, 2 no lick. Broadcast over 80 bins | `behavior_lick_directions`, `behavior_report` | validated against first post-go lick (see sanity checks) |\n| `outcome` | `output[1]` \"outcome\" | 0 ignore, 1 miss, 2 hit. Broadcast | `behavior_report` | |\n| `early_lick` | `output[2]` \"early_lick\" | 0 no, 1 yes. Broadcast | `behavior_early_report` | |\n| `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` (`tongue_y`, `tongue_likelihood`) | `output[3]` \"tongue_y_position\" | per 50 ms bin: mean `y` over frames with likelihood > 0.5; 3 if no visible frame in the bin; else 0/1/2 by session-wide 40th/60th percentiles of visible `y` | `align_markers_between_lims` | time-varying |\n| `general/subject/subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | | | |\n| `units/anno_name` + `electrodes.x` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | CCF name → one of 14 major regions (`region_map.py`); hemisphere from `x >= 5700` ⇒ left | `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | region names are `\"left ALM\"` etc. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session curation** — keep sessions with behavioural performance > 65 % on\n   control (no-photostim), non-early-lick trials, and ≥ 50 correct lick-left and\n   ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials. *Rationale*: this is the explicit session-selection\n   rule in the data paper's STAR Methods; applying it reproduces the reported 84 %\n   mean correct rate (we get 83.5 %). 148 of 174 sessions survive.\n2. **Neuron curation** — `classification == 'good'` (the QC-classifier \"good units\"\n   used throughout both papers), plus a mappable CCF annotation. Neurons with zero\n   spikes in the extracted windows across the whole session are dropped (they carry\n   no information and match `check_fr`'s intent).\n3. **Trial curation** — drop auto-water and free-water trials (reference excludes\n   them; their `outcome` is not a behavioural report). Keep photostim, early-lick and\n   ignore trials because they are required decoder inputs/outputs.\n4. **No 2 Hz firing-rate threshold** — see Step 4 table.\n5. **Alignment / binning** — go cue = 0; 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins spanning\n   [−2.5, +1.5) s; firing rate in Hz.\n6. **Tone onset** = the last sample-epoch onset at or before the go cue. Early-lick\n   trials replay the sample epoch, so this is the last time the instruction tones\n   were actually played before the go cue.\n7. **Tongue visibility threshold** = DeepLabCut likelihood > 0.5. The likelihood\n   distribution is strongly bimodal (median ≈ 6e-5 for occluded frames vs ≈ 1.0 when\n   visible), so any threshold in [0.1, 0.99] gives essentially the same labels\n   (fraction visible 0.1045 at 0.99 vs 0.1059 at 0.5); sensitivity checked in Step 10.\n8. **Tongue percentiles computed per session over all visible frames of the whole\n   session** — literal reading of \"over the session\"; using only visible frames is\n   required because `y` is meaningless when the tongue is occluded.\n9. **Per-trial outputs are broadcast over time** so that all four outputs share one\n   (4, 80) array, as required by the format (tongue position is time-varying).\n10. **Bins outside the recorded trial interval are left as 0 Hz.** The published\n    dataset simply has no spikes there; no imputation is possible and masking is not\n    representable in the format. Documented as a known dataset property.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Good-unit fraction ≈ 25.9 % of all Kilosort clusters (paper).\n- [ ] Per-region good-unit totals vs paper (ALM/Str/Thal/Mid/Med).\n- [ ] Median good units per session ≈ 393 (paper).\n- [ ] 28 subjects.\n- [ ] Session-selection reproduces mean correct rate ≈ 84 % (range 65–99 %).\n- [ ] Trials/session ≈ 476 after removing early-lick trials.\n- [ ] Choice derived from `outcome`+`instruction` agrees with the direction of the\n      first lick after the go cue.\n- [ ] Trial-averaged firing rate shows a go-cue-locked transient at t = 0.\n- [ ] Tongue \"visible\" fraction peaks right after the go cue and is ≈ 0 before it.\n- [ ] `time_from_tone_onset` is 1.85 s at the go-cue bin for ordinary trials.\n- [ ] `photostim_on` is non-zero only inside the delay epoch (always ends before go).\n- [ ] Output class fractions match the raw NWB trial-table fractions.\n- [ ] Independent spot-checks (`np.allclose`) of neural/input/output against raw NWB.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` (+ `/app/region_map.py` for the CCF annotation → major-region\ntable). Runs as `python -u /app/convert_data.py <outfile> [--full|--sample]\n[--show-processing] [--jobs N]`.\n\nPipeline per session (`process_session`):\n1. `read_session` — one pass over the NWB file, pulling the trials table, the\n   behavioural events, the side-view tongue marker and the spike times of the\n   units that pass QC.\n2. Restrict to the trials that the ephys actually observed (`units/obs_intervals`).\n3. Trial curation: drop auto-/free-water trials and trials without full video.\n4. Session curation: performance, ≥50 correct per direction, tongue-tracking sanity.\n5. `bin_spikes` — 80 × 50 ms firing-rate bins aligned to the go cue.\n6. Inputs (`tone_onset_rel`, `photostim_windows`) and outputs\n   (`choice_from_trials`, outcome/early-lick maps, `tongue_classes`).\n7. Pack into per-trial arrays.\n\nSessions are processed in parallel with a `ProcessPoolExecutor`.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- A naive implementation bins spikes per (unit, trial) with `np.searchsorted` over\n  81 bin edges — ~87 M searchsorted probes per session.\n- The same applies to the 300 Hz video: ~1 M frames × 80 bins per session.\n- Reading all of `units/spike_times` (up to 90 MB) per session.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- **Single-pass histogram for the whole session.** Because the go cues are ≥4.58 s\n  apart (asserted) the 4 s trial windows never overlap, so every spike belongs to\n  at most one (trial, bin). The trial index comes from one `np.searchsorted` over\n  the 136-to-800-element window-start array, the bin index from integer division,\n  and the whole (unit, trial, bin) tensor from a single `np.bincount`. O(n_spikes)\n  instead of O(n_units · n_trials · n_bins · log n_spikes).\n- The same trick bins the video frames for the tongue output.\n- Only the spike ranges of QC-passing units are sliced out of `spike_times`.\n- `float32` neural arrays, `int64` outputs.\n- 24 worker processes.\n\nResult: **0.11 s per NWB file wall-clock** (≈1.0 s of CPU per session), 19 s for\nall 174 files; 36 s including writing the 9.45 GB pickle.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n→ `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`, `/app/sample_data.pkl`,\n`processing_440956_20190209T150135.png`, `processing_440956_20190210T155629.png`.\n`--sample` scans the first files until two sessions pass the session-selection\ncriteria (the first two files of the archive are both rejected), so the sample is\na usable two-session mini-dataset.\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 (of the first 8 files scanned) |\n| Neurons (total) | 757 |\n| Neurons / session | 205, 552 (median 378) |\n| Subjects | 1 (440956) |\n| Sessions / subject | 2 |\n| Trials (total) | 723 |\n| Trials / session | 517, 206 |\n| Brain regions | 12 |\n| `time_from_tone_onset_s` range | [-0.625, 4.467] s |\n| `photostim_on` | fraction of time bins on = 0.037 |\n| `choice` distribution | [0.498, 0.479, 0.024] |\n| `outcome` distribution | [0.024, 0.062, 0.914] |\n| `early_lick` distribution | [0.992, 0.008] |\n| `tongue_y_position` distribution | [0.099, 0.050, 0.099, 0.751] |\n\nNote session `440956_20190210T155629` has 582 behavioural trials but only 206 with\nephys coverage — exactly the `obs_intervals` restriction from Step 2.\n\n### Processing Plots Review\nEach `processing_<session>.png` has 12 panels covering every processing stage. What\nthey show (no anomalies found):\n- **Population PSTH**: flat baseline, a bump beginning exactly at the median tone\n  onset (−1.85 s) and ending at the end of the sample epoch (−1.2 s), and a large\n  transient starting exactly at t = 0. Independent confirmation that the neural\n  data are correctly aligned to the go cue.\n- **Trial-interval coverage**: the fraction of trials with no population spikes is\n  0 for every bin up to +0.6 s, then rises to the fraction of error trials — the\n  `stop_time` truncation described in Step 2, and nothing else.\n- **Neural heat map** for one trial: firing rates are sensible (0 – a few hundred Hz).\n- **input 0**: a straight ramp of slope 1 crossing zero at the tone onset.\n- **input 1**: photostimulation confined to the delay epoch, always ending at or\n  before the go cue; a raster over trials shows the ~20 % randomly interleaved\n  stimulation trials.\n- **raw photostim event histogram**: on at −1.2 s or −0.5 s, off 0.5 s later.\n- **tongue raw y vs derived class** for one trial: the step trace of the derived\n  class follows the raw visible-frame positions and switches at the 40th/60th\n  percentile lines; class 3 exactly where there are no visible frames.\n- **tongue class raster / class fractions over time**: ≈0 visible before the go cue,\n  rising abruptly at t = 0 — the licking response. Correct temporal alignment of\n  the video stream against the neural stream.\n- **per-trial outputs, class fractions, PSTH split by choice**: lick-left, lick-right\n  and no-lick trials have clearly different population responses.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n|---|---|\n| single `np.bincount` histogram instead of per-(unit,trial) `searchsorted` | ≈100× on the binning step |\n| same for the 300 Hz video frames | ≈50× on the tongue step |\n| slice only QC-passing units out of `spike_times` | ≈2× on I/O |\n| 24 worker processes | ≈10× wall-clock |\n\n| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n|---|---|---|\n| read NWB | 0.45 s | 78 s CPU |\n| bin spikes | 0.50 s | 87 s CPU |\n| inputs + outputs | 0.02 s | 3 s CPU |\n| pack | 0.02 s | 3 s CPU |\n| **total (24 workers)** | **0.11 s wall** | **19 s** + 17 s to write the pickle |\n\nThe two sample sessions are close to the dataset median in neurons and trials, and\nthe full run (36 s) confirmed the estimate. No further optimisation needed.\n\n### Format verification (`--verify-only`)\n`/app/verification_sample_out.txt`: **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\nAll trials have T = 80; input range and output distributions as tabulated above.\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl` → `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n\n### Format Validation\n- Errors: None\n- Warnings: None\n\n### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions / 723 trials)\nTraining loss falls monotonically from 11.9 (epoch 1) to 0.49 (epoch 200);\ntest loss 0.53 — no divergence, no overfitting collapse.\n\n| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc |\n|--------|--------|-----------------------|--------------------------|\n| choice | 0.333 | 0.788 | 0.667 |\n| outcome | 0.333 | 0.779 | 0.558 |\n| early_lick | 0.500 | 0.975 | 0.960 |\n| tongue_y_position | 0.250 | 0.636 | 0.575 |\n\nEvery output is above chance. It is a *binary* variable, for which\n1.5× chance is 0.75 balanced accuracy — a strong effect, not a weak one; the same\nratio for a 4-class variable would be 0.375. Two further facts rule out a\nconversion bug:\n- 99.1 % of early-lick trials contain at least one recorded lick inside the\n  extracted window, and their pre-go-cue tongue-visible fraction is 26.3 % versus\n  4.7 % on non-early trials — the event really is inside the window and really is\n  visible in the behavioural data.\n- Resolved in time, `early_lick` decoding **peaks at 0.818 during the sample epoch\n  and 0.812 during the delay** (exactly when an early lick can occur) and falls to\n  0.696 in the response epoch (after the go cue, when every mouse is licking and the\n  distinction is no longer visible). That is the physiologically correct time course;\n  the whole-trial average of 0.752 is diluted by the response epoch.\n\n### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to the papers\nNeither reference paper trains this decoder, so the comparable quantities are the\nchoice decoders of the data paper. To compare like with like, the 3-way `choice`\npredictions were restricted to lick-left/lick-right trials and scored as binary\n(`/app/cache/time_resolved_accuracy.py`):\n\n| Quantity | Paper | This conversion | Comment |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Single-session population choice decoding, all neurons in an area, mean over late delay [−0.6, −0.1] s (Fig. S6B) | dots scattered ≈0.6–0.9, mean ≈0.75 for ALM and lower for other areas | **0.691** over [−0.6, 0) s | Matching regime (one session's neurons, late delay). Our sessions mix all 14 regions, most far less choice-selective than ALM |\n| Population choice decoding at t = −0.1 s vs neuron count (Fig. S6E) | 0.55–0.6 at 20 neurons, rising with neuron count | **0.699** at t = −0.075 s | In range |\n| Pseudo-population choice decoding, 200 ALM neurons pooled across mice, 200 ms causal window, end of delay (Fig. 6D) | ≈0.90 (ALM); ≈0.6–0.75 for striatum/thalamus/midbrain/medulla | 0.691 late delay, **0.927 peak (response epoch)** | Not directly comparable — see below |\n| Single-neuron choice AUC threshold used to call a neuron \"choice-modulated\" (methodpaper Fig. 7) | 0.65 | — | population decoding, not per neuron |\n| Video→spike-rate prediction (methodpaper Fig. 2) | R² 0.2–0.8 | — | regression, not classification |\n\nWhy the Fig. 6D number is higher than our late-delay value, and why this is not a\nconversion defect:\n1. **Neuron pooling.** Fig. 6D builds 200-neuron pseudo-populations *per brain area*\n   by hierarchical bootstrapping across mice and sessions. Our decoder sees one real\n   session at a time, projected to 100 dimensions, with all regions mixed. The\n   paper's own Fig. S6E shows accuracy at t = −0.1 s climbing steeply with neuron\n   count; the single-session version of the same measurement (Fig. S6B) is\n   substantially lower and is what our 0.691 matches.\n2. **Smoothing.** The paper uses 200 ms causal windows; the task here specifies\n   50 ms bins, i.e. a quarter of the spikes per sample.\n3. **Trial composition.** The paper excludes photostimulation trials; we must keep\n   them (photostimulation is a required decoder input). Bilateral ALM inactivation\n   drops behavioural performance from 83.2 % to 71.7 % and is designed to disrupt\n   the delay-epoch choice code, so those 20 % of trials lower late-delay choice\n   decoding by construction.\n4. **A single shared decoder** is fitted jointly across 136 sessions and all 80 time\n   points, rather than a separate nested-cross-validated logistic regression per\n   brain area and per time point.\n\nCrucially, the **peak binary choice accuracy of 0.927** (in the response epoch)\nexceeds the paper's best late-delay ALM value, which rules out any temporal\nmisalignment or label error: the information is there, it simply emerges with the\ntask's own time course.\n\n### Time-resolved validation accuracy (`/app/cache/time_resolved_accuracy_out.txt`)\n| Output | pre-sample [−2.5,−1.85) | sample [−1.85,−1.2) | delay [−1.2,0) | response [0,1.5) | peak |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| choice (3-way) | 0.534 | 0.623 | 0.660 | **0.790** | 0.874 |\n| choice (binary L/R) | 0.531 | 0.639 | 0.678 | **0.838** | 0.927 |\n| outcome | 0.556 | 0.575 | 0.591 | **0.795** | 0.881 |\n| early_lick | 0.736 | **0.819** | **0.812** | 0.696 | 0.845 |\n| tongue_y_position | 0.681 | 0.666 | 0.644 | 0.589 | 0.698 |\n\nEvery time course is the physiologically expected one, which is the strongest\navailable evidence that the streams are correctly aligned:\n- choice and outcome are near chance before the instruction tone, rise through the\n  sample and delay epochs, and jump after the go cue;\n- early-lick decoding peaks precisely in the sample/delay epochs where early licks\n  occur;\n- tongue position is decodable throughout (licking also occurs before the go cue on\n  early-lick trials and at the start of the window, which overlaps the previous\n  trial's consumption licking).\n\n### Check 3 — Train vs validation gap\nRatios train/validation: choice 1.06, outcome 1.09, early_lick 1.05,\ntongue_y_position 1.04 — all far below the 1.5 threshold.
