Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING WHEN APPLICABLE**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference papers and code, except where the decoder input and output specifications or training a neural decoder require otherwise. You will be assessed on the statistics and decoder accuracy of your converted data, and on whether an expert would consider each of your decisions well justified.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository when applicable. This requires examining the provided papers, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- **Papers**:\n  - Data were first presented in \"Brain-wide neural activity underlying memory-guided movement\". It is in the file `/app/datapaper.pdf`\n  - A recent paper that uses this dataset is \"Brain-wide analysis reveals movement encoding structured across and within brain areas\". It is in the file `/app/methodpaper.pdf`\n  - A white paper about spike sorting and quality control is in the file `/app/ChenLiuEtAl2023_SpikeSortingQC.pdf`\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code**:\n  - `/app/code` contains code from `/app/methodpaper.pdf`.\n- **Data** from the papers are in the directory `/app/data`.\n- **Documentation** on the NWB file format is at https://nwb-schema.readthedocs.io. \n\n## Decoder Task\n\n- Temporally align based on **Go cue onset**. \n- Extract **2.5 s before to 1.5 s after** the go cue for each trial\n- Use **50-ms-width bins** for computing firing rates. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from **tone onset** in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n- Whether **photostimulation** is on at every time point (discrete, time-varying)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Lick direction **choice** (left, right, no lick, per-trial)\n- **Outcome** (ignore, miss, hit, per-trial)\n- **Early lick** (no, yes, per-trial)\n- **Tongue y-position**, per-session discretization:\n  - 0: < 40th percentile of y-position over the session\n  - 1: 40th to 60th percentile of y-position over the session\n  - 2: > 60th percentile of y-position over the session\n  - 3: not visible\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference papers and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder.\n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. Perform all of the following checks:\n\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers' accuracy for each variable\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n5. sel=slice(None)):\n   223→    \"\"\"Correct rate on control trials excluding early licks (datapaper definition).\"\"\"\n   224→    ps = d['photostim_power'][sel] != 'N/A'\n   225→    ctrl = (~ps) & (d['early_lick'][sel] == 'no early')\n   226→    hit = d['outcome'][sel] == 'hit'\n   227→    miss = d['outcome'][sel] == 'miss'\n   228→    m = ctrl & (hit | miss)\n   229→    perf = hit[m].sum() / m.sum() if m.sum() > 0 else 0.0\n   230→    n_left = int((hit & ctrl & (d['instruction'][sel] == 'left')).sum())\n   231→    n_right = int((hit & ctrl & (d['instruction'][sel] == 'right')).sum())\n   232→    return float(perf), n_left, n_right\n   233→\n   234→\n   235→def video_coverage(d, win_start):\n   236→    \"\"\"Fraction of the [-2.5, +1.5] s window covered by video frames, per trial.\"\"\"\n   237→    ts = d['tongue_t']\n   238→    lo = np.searchsorted(ts, win_start)\n   239→    hi = np.searchsorted(ts, win_start + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH)\n   240→    expected = (N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH) / VIDEO_DT\n   241→    return (hi - lo) / expected\n   242→\n   243→\n   244→# ----------------------------------------------------------------- core processing\n   245→def bin_spikes(unit_spikes, win_start):\n   246→    \"\"\"Bin spike times of all units into (n_units, n_trials, N_BINS) firing rates.\n   247→\n   248→    `win_start` is the session-time of the first bin edge for each trial\n   249→    (= go cue - 2.5 s).  Windows are guaranteed non-overlapping (asserted by the\n   250→    caller), so every spike falls in at most one window and the whole session can\n   251→    be binned with a single `np.bincount`.\n   252→    \"\"\"\n   253→    n_units = len(unit_spikes)\n   254→    n_trials = len(win_start)\n   255→    win_end = win_start + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH\n   256→\n   257→    if n_units == 0:\n   258→        return np.zeros((0, n_trials, N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n   259→\n   260→    lens = np.array([len(s) for s in unit_spikes])\n   261→    if lens.sum() == 0:\n   262→        return np.zeros((n_units, n_trials, N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n   263→    t = np.concatenate(unit_spikes) if n_units > 1 else unit_spikes[0]\n   264→    uidx = np.repeat(np.arange(n_units), lens)\n   265→\n   266→    ti = np.searchsorted(win_start, t, side='right') - 1\n   267→    valid = (ti >= 0) & (ti < n_trials)\n   268→    ti_c = np.where(valid, ti, 0)\n   269→    valid &= t < win_end[ti_c]\n   270→\n   271→    t = t[valid]\n   272→    uidx = uidx[valid]\n   273→    ti = ti[valid]\n   274→    b = ((t - win_start[ti]) / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.int64)\n   275→    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n   276→\n   277→    flat = (uidx * n_trials + ti) * N_BINS + b\n   278→    counts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_trials * N_BINS)\n   279→    counts = counts.reshape(n_units, n_trials, N_BINS)\n   280→    return (counts / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.float32)\n   281→\n   282→\n   283→def tone_onset_rel(d):\n   284→    \"\"\"Time of the last sample-epoch (tone) onset at or before each go cue,\n   285→    expressed relative to the go cue (so always <= 0).\"\"\"\n   286→    si = np.searchsorted(d['sample_start'], d['go_time'], side='right') - 1\n   287→    assert (si >= 0).all(), 'a trial has no preceding sample epoch'\n   288→    return d['sample_start'][si] - d['go_time']\n   289→\n   290→\n   291→def photostim_windows(d):\n   292→    \"\"\"Per-trial [on, off] photostimulation times relative to the go cue.\n   293→\n   294→    NaN for trials without photostimulation.\n   295→    \"\"\"\n   296→    n = len(d['go_time'])\n   297→    on = np.full(n, np.nan)\n   298→    off = np.full(n, np.nan)\n   299→    if len(d['stim_on']) == 0:\n   300→        return on, off\n   301→    ti = np.searchsorted(d['trial_start'], d['stim_on'], side='right') - 1\n   302→    ok = (ti >= 0) & (ti < n)\n   303→    ti, s_on, s_off = ti[ok], d['stim_on'][ok], d['stim_off'][ok]\n   304→    # keep the first stimulation event of each trial (there is exactly one)\n   305→    on[ti] = s_on - d['go_time'][ti]\n   306→    off[ti] = s_off - d['go_time'][ti]\n   307→    return on, off\n   308→\n   309→\n   310→def tongue_bin_position(d, win_start):\n   311→    \"\"\"Mean visible tongue-y per (trial, bin), and a mask of bins where the\n   312→    tongue was visible at all.\n   313→\n   314→    A video frame counts as \"tongue visible\" when its DeepLabCut likelihood\n   315→    exceeds `TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH`; the likelihood distribution is strongly\n   316→    bimodal so the exact threshold is immaterial.  Frames where the tongue is\n   317→    occluded carry no position information (the reference code replaces them by\n   318→    the session mean), so they are excluded from the bin average.\n   319→    \"\"\"\n   320→    n_trials = len(win_start)\n   321→    ybar = np.zeros((n_trials, N_BINS))\n   322→    has = np.zeros((n_trials, N_BINS), dtype=bool)\n   323→\n   324→    visible = d['tongue_lik'] > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH\n   325→    if visible.sum() == 0:\n   326→        return ybar, has\n   327→\n   328→    vt = d['tongue_t'][visible]\n   329→    vy = d['tongue_y'][visible]\n   330→    win_end = win_start + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH\n   331→    ti = np.searchsorted(win_start, vt, side='right') - 1\n   332→    ok = (ti >= 0) & (ti < n_trials)\n   333→    ti_c = np.where(ok, ti, 0)\n   334→    ok &= vt < win_end[ti_c]\n   335→    vt, vy, ti = vt[ok], vy[ok], ti[ok]\n   336→    if len(vt) == 0:\n   337→        return ybar, has\n   338→\n   339→    b = ((vt - win_start[ti]) / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.int64)\n   340→    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n   341→    flat = ti * N_BINS + b\n   342→    nvis = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_trials * N_BINS).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS)\n   343→    ysum = np.bincount(flat, weights=vy,\n   344→                       minlength=n_trials * N_BINS).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS)\n   345→    has = nvis > 0\n   346→    ybar[has] = ysum[has] / nvis[has]\n   347→    return ybar, has\n   348→\n   349→\n   350→def tongue_classes(d, win_start):\n   351→    \"\"\"(n_trials, N_BINS) int array of tongue-y classes 0/1/2/3.\n   352→\n   353→    Discretisation thresholds are the 40th and 60th percentiles of the\n   354→    tongue y-position *of this session* (bins in which the tongue is visible).\n   355→    \"\"\"\n   356→    ybar, has = tongue_bin_position(d, win_start)\n   357→    out = np.full(ybar.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)\n   358→    if has.sum() < 2:\n   359→        return out, np.nan, np.nan\n   360→    p_lo, p_hi = np.percentile(ybar[has], [TONGUE_LOW_PCT, TONGUE_HIGH_PCT])\n   361→    cls = np.where(ybar < p_lo, 0, np.where(ybar > p_hi, 2, 1))\n   362→    out[has] = cls[has]\n   363→    return out, float(p_lo), float(p_hi)\n   364→\n   365→\n   366→def choice_from_trials(d):\n   367→    \"\"\"0 = left, 1 = right, 2 = no lick.\"\"\"\n   368→    ch = np.full(len(d['outcome']), 2, dtype=np.int64)\n   369→    hit = d['outcome'] == 'hit'\n   370→    miss = d['outcome'] == 'miss'\n   371→    left = d['instruction'] == 'left'\n   372→    ch[hit & left] = 0\n   373→    ch[hit & ~left] = 1\n   374→    ch[miss & left] = 1      # error on a lick-left instruction -> licked right\n   375→    ch[miss & ~left] = 0\n   376→    return ch\n   377→\n   378→\n   379→def process_session(path, want_debug=False):\n   380→    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file.  Returns a dict (or None if the session is rejected).\"\"\"\n   381→    t0 = time.time()\n   382→    d = read_session(path)\n   383→    t_read = time.time() - t0\n   384→\n   385→    go = d['go_time']\n   386→    assert len(go) == len(d['trial_start'])\n   387→    assert np.all(np.diff(go) > (T_STOP - T_START)), 'trial windows overlap'\n   388→    win_start = go + T_START\n   389→\n   390→    info = dict(session_id=d['session_id'], subject=d['subject'], path=path,\n   391→                n_trials_ephys=int(len(go)),\n   392→                n_trials_behavior=int(d['n_trials_behavior']),\n   393→                n_clusters=int(d['n_all_clusters']),\n   394→                n_good_clusters=int(d['n_good_clusters']))\n   395→\n   396→    # ---- trial curation -----------------------------------------------------\n   397→    # (a) reward not contingent on the animal's choice -> `outcome` is not a\n   398→    #     behavioural report on these trials (reference: get_regular_trial_mask)\n   399→    keep = (~d['auto_water']) & (~d['free_water'])\n   400→    info['n_dropped_water'] = int((~keep).sum())\n   401→    # (b) the behavioural video must cover the whole extracted window, otherwise\n   402→    #     the tongue output cannot be determined (reference: get_bad_trial_inds)\n   403→    cov = video_coverage(d, win_start)\n   404→    keep &= cov >= MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE\n   405→    info['n_dropped_video'] = int(((cov < MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE)).sum())\n   406→    trial_idx = np.where(keep)[0]\n   407→    info['n_trials_kept'] = int(len(trial_idx))\n   408→\n   409→    # ---- session curation ---------------------------------------------------\n   410→    perf, n_left, n_right = session_performance(d, trial_idx)\n   411→    vis_frac = float((d['tongue_lik'] > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH).mean())\n   412→    info.update(performance=perf, n_correct_left=n_left, n_correct_right=n_right,\n   413→                tongue_visible_fraction=vis_frac)\n   414→    if perf <= MIN_PERFORMANCE:\n   415→        info['rejected'] = 'behavioural performance'\n   416→        return None, info\n   417→    if n_left < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION or n_right < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION:\n   418→        info['rejected'] = 'too few correct lick-left / lick-right trials'\n   419→        return None, info\n   420→    if vis_frac > MAX_TONGUE_VISIBLE_FRACTION:\n   421→        info['rejected'] = 'tongue tracking failure (implausible visible fraction)'\n   422→        return None, info\n   423→\n   424→    t1 = time.time()\n   425→    # ---- neural -------------------------------------------------------------\n   426→    fr = bin_spikes(d['unit_spikes'], win_start)          # (n_units, n_trials, 80)\n   427→    fr = fr[:, trial_idx, :]\n   428→    # drop units that are completely silent in every extracted window\n   429→    active = fr.any(axis=(1, 2))\n   430→    fr = fr[active]\n   431→    unit_region = d['unit_region'][active]\n   432→    t_neural = time.time() - t1\n   433→\n   434→    # ---- inputs -------------------------------------------------------------\n   435→    t2 = time.time()\n   436→    tone_rel = tone_onset_rel(d)[trial_idx]               # <= 0\n   437→    time_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS[None, :] - tone_rel[:, None]     # (n_tr, 80)\n   438→\n   439→    on, off = photostim_windows(d)\n   440→    on, off = on[trial_idx], off[trial_idx]\n   441→    has_stim = np.isfinite(on)\n   442→    # A bin is \"on\" when the laser is on at the bin centre, i.e. the binary\n   443→    # laser signal is sampled at the same instants as the bin centres.  Using\n   444→    # interval overlap instead would light up the bin containing the go cue for\n   445→    # a third of the stimulation trials, because the recorded laser-off\n   446→    # timestamp overshoots the go cue by ~1 ms; the photoinhibition always ended\n   447→    # before the go cue (datapaper STAR Methods).  With the centre rule each\n   448→    # 0.5 s stimulation covers exactly 10 bins.\n   449→    stim = np.zeros((len(trial_idx), N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n   450→    if has_stim.any():\n   451→        o = np.where(has_stim, on, np.inf)[:, None]\n   452→        f_ = np.where(has_stim, off, -np.inf)[:, None]\n   453→        stim = ((BIN_CENTERS[None, :] >= o) & (BIN_CENTERS[None, :] <= f_)\n   454→                ).astype(np.float32)\n   455→\n   456→    # ---- outputs ------------------------------------------------------------\n   457→    choice = choice_from_trials(d)[trial_idx]\n   458→    outcome_map = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n   459→    outcome = np.array([outcome_map[o] for o in d['outcome']])[trial_idx]\n   460→    early = (d['early_lick'] == 'early').astype(np.int64)[trial_idx]\n   461→    tongue, p_lo, p_hi = tongue_classes(d, win_start[trial_idx])\n   462→    t_io = time.time() - t2\n   463→\n   464→    # ---- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n   465→    t3 = time.time()\n   466→    n_tr = len(trial_idx)\n   467→    neural = [np.ascontiguousarray(fr[:, i, :]) for i in range(n_tr)]\n   468→    inputs = [np.stack([time_from_tone[i], stim[i]]).astype(np.float32)\n   469→              for i in range(n_tr)]\n   470→    outputs = [np.stack([np.full(N_BINS, choice[i]), np.full(N_BINS, outcome[i]),\n   471→                         np.full(N_BINS, early[i]), tongue[i]]).astype(np.int64)\n   472→               for i in range(n_tr)]\n   473→    t_pack = time.time() - t3\n   474→\n   475→    # provenance: row indices into intervals/trials of the source NWB file, one\n   476→    # per converted trial, so every value can be traced back to the raw data\n   477→    info['raw_trial_index'] = d['ephys_trials'][trial_idx].astype(int).tolist()\n   478→    info.update(n_trials=n_tr, n_neurons=int(fr.shape[0]),\n   479→                tongue_p40=float(p_lo), tongue_p60=float(p_hi),\n   480→                n_photostim_trials=int(has_stim.sum()),\n   481→                t_read=t_read, t_neural=t_neural, t_io=t_io, t_pack=t_pack,\n   482→                t_total=time.time() - t0)\n   483→\n   484→    result = dict(session_id=d['session_id'], subject=d['subject'],\n   485→                  neural=neural, input=inputs, output=outputs,\n   486→                  unit_region=unit_region, info=info)\n   487→\n   488→    if want_debug:\n   489→        result['debug'] = dict(\n   490→            go=go, trial_idx=trial_idx, win_start=win_start,\n   491→            tone_rel=tone_rel, stim_on=on, stim_off=off,\n   492→            tongue_t=d['tongue_t'], tongue_y=d['tongue_y'], tongue_lik=d['tongue_lik'],\n   493→            p_lo=p_lo, p_hi=p_hi, choice=choice, outcome=outcome, early=early,\n   494→            unit_anno=d['unit_anno'][active],\n   495→            trial_start=d['trial_start'], trial_stop=d['trial_stop'],\n   496→        )\n   497→    return result, info\n   498→\n   499→\n   500→# ------------------------------------------------------------------------ plotting\n   501→def make_processing_plot(res, outpath):\n   502→    import matplotlib\n   503→    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n   504→    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n   505→\n   506→    dbg = res['debug']\n   507→    neural = res['neural']\n   508→    inputs = res['input']\n   509→    outputs = res['output']\n   510→    n_tr = len(neural)\n   511→    n_neu = neural[0].shape[0]\n   512→\n   513→    fig, ax = plt.subplots(4, 3, figsize=(22, 18))\n   514→\n   515→    # (0,0) trial-averaged population firing rate + epoch markers\n   516→    mean_fr = np.mean([n.mean(axis=0) for n in neural], axis=0)\n   517→    ax[0, 0].plot(BIN_CENTERS, mean_fr, '-o', ms=3)\n   518→    ax[0, 0].axvline(0, color='r', label='go cue')\n   519→    med_tone = np.median(dbg['tone_rel'])\n   520→    ax[0, 0].axvline(med_tone, color='g', ls='--', label='median tone onset')\n   521→    ax[0, 0].axvline(med_tone + 0.65, color='g', ls=':', label='sample end')\n   522→    ax[0, 0].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n   523→    ax[0, 0].set_ylabel('mean firing rate (Hz)')\n   524→    ax[0, 0].set_title(f'{res[\"session_id\"]}: population PSTH ({n_neu} neurons, {n_tr} trials)')\n   525→    ax[0, 0].legend(fontsize=7)\n   526→\n   527→    # (0,1) fraction of bins with zero spikes across the population (coverage)\n   528→    frac_zero = np.mean([(n.sum(axis=0) == 0) for n in neural], axis=0)\n   529→    ax[0, 1].plot(BIN_CENTERS, frac_zero, '-o', ms=3)\n   530→    ax[0, 1].axvline(0, color='r')\n   531→    ax[0, 1].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n   532→    ax[0, 1].set_ylabel('fraction of trials with no spikes')\n   533→    ax[0, 1].set_title('trial-interval coverage\\n(rises late: error trials end early)')\n   534→\n   535→    # (0,2) raster-like heatmap of one trial\n   536→    it = int(np.argmax([o[1, 0] == 2 for o in outputs]))   # a hit trial\n   537→    im = ax[0, 2].imshow(neural[it], aspect='auto', interpolation='nearest',\n   538→                         extent=[T_START, T_STOP, n_neu, 0], cmap='magma')\n   539→    ax[0, 2].axvline(0, color='c')\n   540→    ax[0, 2].set_title(f'trial {it}: neural (Hz)')\n   541→    ax[0, 2].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n   542→    ax[0, 2].set_ylabel('neuron')\n   543→    plt.colorbar(im, ax=ax[0, 2])\n   544→\n   545→    # (1,0) input 0: time from tone onset\n   546→    for i in range(min(40, n_tr)):\n   547→        ax[1, 0].plot(BIN_CENTERS, inputs[i][0], alpha=0.4)\n   548→    ax[1, 0].axvline(0, color='r')\n   549→    ax[1, 0].axhline(0, color='k', ls=':')\n   550→    ax[1, 0].set_title('input 0: time from tone onset (s)\\n(0 crossing = tone onset)')\n   551→    ax[1, 0].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n   552→\n   553→    # (1,1) input 1: photostim raster\n   554→    stim = np.stack([inp[1] for inp in inputs])\n   555→    ax[1, 1].imshow(stim, aspect='auto', interpolation='nearest',\n   556→                    extent=[T_START, T_STOP, n_tr, 0], cmap='Greys')\n   557→    ax[1, 1].axvline(0, color='r')\n   558→    ax[1, 1].set_title(f'input 1: photostim on ({int((stim.max(axis=1) > 0).sum())} trials)')\n   559→    ax[1, 1].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n   560→    ax[1, 1].set_ylabel('trial')\n   561→\n   562→    # (1,2) check photostim against raw event times\n   563→    ok = np.isfinite(dbg['stim_on'])\n   564→    if ok.any():\n   565→        ax[1, 2].hist(dbg['stim_on'][ok], bins=40, alpha=0.6, label='laser on')\n   566→        ax[1, 2].hist(dbg['stim_off'][ok], bins=40, alpha=0.6, label='laser off')\n   567→    ax[1, 2].axvline(0, color='r', label='go cue')\n   568→    ax[1, 2].set_title('raw photostim event times re. go cue')\n   569→    ax[1, 2].set_xlabel('s'); ax[1, 2].legend(fontsize=7)\n   570→\n   571→    # (2,0) tongue y trace for one trial, raw frames + derived classes\n   572→    itt = int(np.argmax([(o[3] != 3).sum() for o in outputs]))\n   573→    t0 = dbg['win_start'][dbg['trial_idx'][itt]]\n   574→    m = (dbg['tongue_t'] >= t0) & (dbg['tongue_t'] < t0 + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH)\n   575→    tt = dbg['tongue_t'][m] - (t0 - T_START)\n   576→    yy = dbg['tongue_y'][m]\n   577→    vis = dbg['tongue_lik'][m] > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH\n   578→    ax[2, 0].plot(tt[vis], yy[vis], '.', ms=2, label='visible frames')\n   579→    ax[2, 0].axhline(dbg['p_lo'], color='g', ls='--', label='40th pct')\n   580→    ax[2, 0].axhline(dbg['p_hi'], color='m', ls='--', label='60th pct')\n   581→    ax2 = ax[2, 0].twinx()\n   582→    ax2.step(BIN_CENTERS, outputs[itt][3], where='mid', color='k', alpha=0.5)\n   583→    ax2.set_ylabel('class (0-3)')\n   584→    ax[2, 0].axvline(0, color='r')\n   585→    ax[2, 0].set_title(f'trial {itt}: raw tongue y vs derived class')\n   586→    ax[2, 0].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[2, 0].legend(fontsize=7)\n   587→\n   588→    # (2,1) tongue class raster\n   589→    tong = np.stack([o[3] for o in outputs])\n   590→    im = ax[2, 1].imshow(tong, aspect='auto', interpolation='nearest',\n   591→                         extent=[T_START, T_STOP, n_tr, 0], cmap='viridis', vmin=0, vmax=3)\n   592→    ax[2, 1].axvline(0, color='r')\n   593→    ax[2, 1].set_title('output 3: tongue y class (3 = not visible)')\n   594→    ax[2, 1].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[2, 1].set_ylabel('trial')\n   595→    plt.colorbar(im, ax=ax[2, 1])\n   596→\n   597→    # (2,2) class fractions over time\n   598→    for c, lab in enumerate(OUTPUT_VALUES[3]):\n   599→        ax[2, 2].plot(BIN_CENTERS, (tong == c).mean(axis=0), label=lab)\n   600→    ax[2, 2].axvline(0, color='r')\n   601→    ax[2, 2].set_title('tongue class fraction over time\\n(tongue appears after the go cue)')\n   602→    ax[2, 2].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[2, 2].legend(fontsize=7)\n   603→\n   604→    # (3,0) per-trial outputs\n   605→    for k, name in enumerate(OUTPUT_NAMES[:3]):\n   606→        vals = np.array([o[k, 0] for o in outputs])\n   607→        ax[3, 0].plot(vals + 0.1 * k, '.', ms=2, label=name)\n   608→    ax[3, 0].set_title('per-trial outputs across the session')\n   609→    ax[3, 0].set_xlabel('trial'); ax[3, 0].legend(fontsize=7)\n   610→\n   611→    # (3,1) output distributions\n   612→    labels, fracs = [], []\n   613→    for k in range(4):\n   614→        vals = np.concatenate([o[k] for o in outputs])\n   615→        for c, lab in enumerate(OUTPUT_VALUES[k]):\n   616→            labels.append(f'{OUTPUT_NAMES[k]}={lab}')\n   617→            fracs.append((vals == c).mean())\n   618→    ax[3, 1].barh(np.arange(len(fracs)), fracs)\n   619→    ax[3, 1].set_yticks(np.arange(len(fracs)))\n   620→    ax[3, 1].set_yticklabels(labels, fontsize=7)\n   621→    ax[3, 1].set_title('output class fractions (time-point weighted)')\n   622→\n   623→    # (3,2) PSTH split by choice\n   624→    ch = np.array([o[0, 0] for o in outputs])\n   625→    for c, lab in enumerate(OUTPUT_VALUES[0]):\n   626→        m = ch == c\n   627→        if m.sum() == 0:\n   628→            continue\n   629→        ax[3, 2].plot(BIN_CENTERS, np.mean([neural[i].mean(axis=0)\n   630→                                            for i in np.where(m)[0]], axis=0), label=lab)\n   631→    ax[3, 2].axvline(0, color='r')\n   632→    ax[3, 2].set_title('population PSTH by choice')\n   633→    ax[3, 2].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[3, 2].legend(fontsize=7)\n   634→\n   635→    fig.tight_layout()\n   636→    fig.savefig(outpath, dpi=110)\n   637→    plt.close(fig)\n   638→    print(f'  wrote {outpath}')\n   639→\n   640→\n   641→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- main\n   642→def _worker(args):\n   643→    path, want_debug = args\n   644→    try:\n   645→        return process_session(path, want_debug=want_debug)\n   646→    except Exception as e:  # keep one bad file from killing the whole run\n   647→        import traceback\n   648→        traceback.print_exc()\n   649→        return None, dict(session_id=session_id_from_path(path), path=path,\n   650→                          rejected=f'error: {e}')\n   651→\n   652→\n   653→def main():\n   654→    ap = (see CONVERSION_NOTES.md for full justification)\n-------------------------------------------------------------\n* Alignment event    : go cue onset (`BehavioralEvents/go_start_times`), matching\n                       the reference preprocessing.\n* Window             : [-2.5, +1.5) s, 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins.\n* Neural             : firing rate in Hz (spike count / bin width), as in the\n Photostim,\n                       early-lick and no-response trials are *kept* because they\n                       are required decoder inputs/outputs.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport glob\nimport json\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport sys\nimport time\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport h5py\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\nfrom region_map import annotation_to_region  # noqa: E402\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- config\nDATA_DIR = '/app/data'\n\nBIN_WIDTH = 0.05          # s, decoder spec: 50 ms bins\nT_START = -2.5            # s relative to go cue, decoder spec\nT_STOP = 1.5              # s relative to go cue, decoder spec\nN_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN_WIDTH))   # 80\n\nTONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH = 0.5   # DLC likelihood is bimodal (~1e-5 vs ~1.0)\nTONGUE_LOW_PCT = 40.0\nTONGUE_HIGH_PCT = 60.0\n\n# Session selection (datapaper STAR Methods)\nMIN_PERFORMANCE = 0.65\nMIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION = 50\n\n# Video curation.  A trial needs video over its whole [-2.5, +1.5] s window for\n# the tongue output to be meaningful; the reference code applies the analogous\n# check in Sherlock/align_markers.get_bad_trial_inds.  The expected number of\n# 300 Hz frames in the window is 4.0 / 0.0034 = 1176.\nVIDEO_DT = 0.0034\nMIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE = 0.9\n# The tongue is only outside the mouth while licking (median 11 % of a session,\n# 99th percentile 25 %).  A session whose DeepLabCut tongue likelihood is high\n# almost everywhere is a tracking failure, not a licking mouse.\nMAX_TONGUE_VISIBLE_FRACTION = 0.5\n\n# hemisphere split on the CCF ML axis, as in the reference code\n# (helper_get_neuron_id_area: `left` is ccf_x >= 5700)\nML_MIDLINE = 5700.0\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_tone_onset_s', 'photostim_on']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['choice', 'outcome', 'early_lick', 'tongue_y_position']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [\n    ['left', 'right', 'no lick'],\n    ['ignore', 'miss', 'hit'],\n    ['no', 'yes'],\n    ['<40th pct', '40th-60th pct', '>60th pct', 'not visible'],\n]\n\nBIN_EDGES = T_START + BIN_WIDTH * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)   # units' `obs_intervals` (and `is_good_trials`) cover only a prefix of\n        # the trials table.  Spikes simply do not exist for the remaining\n        # trials, so they must be dropped.  `obs_intervals` rows are exactly\n        # the [start_time, stop_time] of the covered trials.\n        # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n        oi_index = u['obs_intervals_index'][:]\n        n_int = np.diff(np.concatenate([[0], oi_index]))\n        assert n_int.min() == n_int.max(), 'units disagree on observed trials'\n        obs = u['obs_intervals'][0:oi_index[0]]\n        ephys_trials = np.searchsorted(trial_start_all, obs[:, 0])\n        assert starts = np.concatenate([[0], st_index[:-1]])\n        spike_times = u['spike_times'][:]\n        unit_spikes = [spike_times[starts[i]:st_index[i]] for i in idx]\n        d['unit_spikes'] = session selection\ndef session_performance(d, sel=slice(None)):\n    \"\"\"Correct rate on control trials excluding early licks (datapaper definition).\"\"\"\n    ps = d['photostim_power'][sel] != 'N/A'\n    ctrl = (~ps) & (d['early_lick'][sel] == 'no early')\n    hit = d['outcome'][sel] == 'hit'\n    miss = d['outcome'][sel] == 'miss'\n    m = ctrl & (hit | miss)\n    perf = hit[m].sum() / m.sum() if m.sum() > 0 else 0.0\n    n_left = int((hit & ctrl & (d['instruction'][sel] == 'left')).sum())\n    n_right = int((hit & ctrl & (d['instruction'][sel] == 'right')).sum())\n    return float(perf), n_left, n_right\n\n\ndef video_coverage(d, win_start):\n    \"\"\"Fraction of the [-2.5, +1.5] s window covered by video frames, per trial.\"\"\"\n    ts = d['tongue_t']\n    lo = np.searchsorted(ts, win_start)\n    hi = np.searchsorted(ts, win_start + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH)\n    expected = (N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH) / VIDEO_DT\n    return (hi - lo) / expected\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------- core processing\ndef bin_spikes(unit_spikes, win_start):\n    \"\"\"Bin spike times of all units into (n_units, n_trials, N_BINS) firing rates.\n\n    `win_start` is the session-time of the first bin edge for each trial\n    (= go cue - 2.5 s).  Windows are guaranteed non-overlapping (asserted by the\n    caller), so every spike falls in at most one window and the whole session can\n    be binned with a single `np.bincount`.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_units = len(unit_spikes)\n    n_trials = len(win_start)\n    win_end = win_start + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH\n\n    if n_units == 0:\n        return np.zeros((0, n_trials, N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n\n    lens = np.array([len(s) for s in unit_spikes])\n    if lens.sum() == 0:\n        return np.zeros((n_units, n_trials, N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n    t = np.concatenate(unit_spikes) if n_units > 1 else unit_spikes[0]\n    uidx = np.repeat(np.arange(n_units), lens)\n\n    ti = np.searchsorted(win_start, t, side='right') - 1\n    valid = (ti >= 0) & (ti < n_trials)\n    ti_c = np.where(valid, ti, 0)\n    valid &= t < win_end[ti_c]\n\n    t = t[valid]\n    uidx = uidx[valid]\n    ti = ti[valid]\n    b = ((t - win_start[ti]) / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.int64)\n    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n\n    flat = (uidx * n_trials + ti) * N_BINS + b\n    counts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_trials * N_BINS)\n    counts = counts.reshape(n_units, n_trials, N_BINS)\n    return (counts / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.float32)\n\n\ndef tone_onset_rel(d):\n    \"\"\"Time of the last sample-epoch (tone) onset at or before each go cue,\n    expressed relative to the go cue (so always <= 0).\"\"\"\n    si = np.searchsorted(d['sample_start'], d['go_time'], side='right') - 1\n    assert (si >= 0).all(), 'a trial has no preceding sample epoch'\n    return d['sample_start'][si] - d['go_time']\n\n\ndef photostim_windows(d):\n    \"\"\"Per-trial [on, off] photostimulation times relative to the go cue.\n\n    NaN for trials without photostimulation.\n    \"\"\"\n    n = len(d['go_time'])\n    on = np.full(n, np.nan)\n    off = np.full(n, np.nan)\n    if len(d['stim_on']) == 0:\n        return on, off\n    ti = np.searchsorted(d['trial_start'], d['stim_on'], side='right') - 1\n    ok = (ti >= 0) & (ti < n)\n    ti, s_on, s_off = ti[ok], d['stim_on'][ok], d['stim_off'][ok]\n    # keep the first stimulation event of each trial (there is exactly one)\n    on[ti] = s_on - d['go_time'][ti]\n    off[ti] = s_off - d['go_time'][ti]\n    return on, off\n\n\ndef tongue_bin_position(d, win_start):\n    \"\"\"Mean visible tongue-y per (trial, bin), and a mask of bins where the\n    tongue was visible at all.\n\n    A video frame counts as \"tongue visible\" when its DeepLabCut likelihood\n    exceeds `TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH`; the likelihood distribution is strongly\n    bimodal so the exact threshold is immaterial.  Frames where the tongue is\n    occluded carry no position information (the reference code replaces them by\n    the session mean), so they are excluded from the bin average.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(win_start)\n    ybar = np.zeros((n_trials, N_BINS))\n    has = np.zeros((n_trials, N_BINS), dtype=bool)\n\n    visible = d['tongue_lik'] > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH\n    if visible.sum() == 0:\n        return ybar, has\n\n    vt = d['tongue_t'][visible]\n    vy = d['tongue_y'][visible]\n    win_end = win_start + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH\n    ti = np.searchsorted(win_start, vt, side='right') - 1\n    ok = (ti >= 0) & (ti < n_trials)\n    ti_c = np.where(ok, ti, 0)\n    ok &= vt < win_end[ti_c]\n    vt, vy, ti = vt[ok], vy[ok], ti[ok]\n    if len(vt) == 0:\n        return ybar, has\n\n    b = ((vt - win_start[ti]) / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.int64)\n    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n    flat = ti * N_BINS + b\n    nvis = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_trials * N_BINS).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS)\n    ysum = np.bincount(flat, weights=vy,\n                       minlength=n_trials * N_BINS).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS)\n    has = nvis > 0\n    ybar[has] = ysum[has] / nvis[has]\n    return ybar, has\n\n\ndef tongue_classes(d, win_start):\n    \"\"\"(n_trials, N_BINS) int array of tongue-y classes 0/1/2/3.\n\n    Discretisation thresholds are the 40th and 60th percentiles of the\n    tongue y-position *of this session* (bins in which the tongue is visible).\n    \"\"\"\n    ybar, has = tongue_bin_position(d, win_start)\n    out = np.full(ybar.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)\n    if has.sum() < 2:\n        return out, np.nan, np.nan\n    p_lo, p_hi = np.percentile(ybar[has], [TONGUE_LOW_PCT, TONGUE_HIGH_PCT])\n    cls = np.where(ybar < p_lo, 0, np.where(ybar > p_hi, 2, 1))\n    out[has] = cls[has]\n    return out, float(p_lo), float(p_hi)\n\n\ndef choice_from_trials(d):\n    \"\"\"0 = left, 1 = right, 2 = no lick.\"\"\"\n    ch = np.full(len(d['outcome']), 2, dtype=np.int64)\n    hit = d['outcome'] == 'hit'\n    miss = d['outcome'] == 'miss'\n    left = d['instruction'] == 'left'\n    ch[hit & left] = 0\n    ch[hit & ~left] = 1\n    ch[miss & left] = 1      # error on a lick-left instruction -> licked right\n    ch[miss & ~left] = 0\n    return ch\n\n\ndef process_session(path, want_debug=False):\n    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file.  Returns a dict (or None if the session is rejected).\"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n    d = read_session(path)\n    t_read = time.time() - t0\n\n    go = d['go_time']\n    assert len(go) == len(d['trial_start'])\n    assert np.all(np.diff(go) > (T_STOP - T_START)), 'trial windows overlap'\n    win_start = go + T_START\n\n    info = dict(session_id=d['session_id'], subject=d['subject'], path=path,\n                n_trials_ephys=int(len(go)),\n                n_trials_behavior=int(d['n_trials_behavior']),\n                n_clusters=int(d['n_all_clusters']),\n                n_good_clusters=int(d['n_good_clusters']))\n\n    # ---- trial curation -----------------------------------------------------\n    # (a) reward not contingent on the animal's choice -> `outcome` is not a\n    #     behavioural report on these trials (reference: get_regular_trial_mask)\n    keep = (~d['auto_water']) & (~d['free_water'])\n    info['n_dropped_water'] = int((~keep).sum())\n    # (b) the behavioural video must cover the whole extracted window, otherwise\n    #     the tongue output cannot be determined (reference: get_bad_trial_inds)\n    cov = video_coverage(d, win_start)\n    keep &= cov >= MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE\n    info['n_dropped_video'] = int(((cov < MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE)).sum())\n    trial_idx = np.where(keep)[0]\n    info['n_trials_kept'] = int(len(trial_idx))\n\n    # ---- session curation ---------------------------------------------------\n    perf, n_left, n_right = session_performance(d, trial_idx)\n    vis_frac = float((d['tongue_lik'] > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH).mean())\n    info.update(performance=perf, n_correct_left=n_left, n_correct_right=n_right,\n                tongue_visible_fraction=vis_frac)\n    if perf <= MIN_PERFORMANCE:\n        info['rejected'] = 'behavioural performance'\n        return None, info\n    if n_left < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION or n_right < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION:\n        info['rejected'] = 'too few correct lick-left / lick-right trials'\n        return None, info\n    if vis_frac > MAX_TONGUE_VISIBLE_FRACTION:\n        info['rejected'] = 'tongue tracking failure (implausible visible fraction)'\n        return None, info\n\n    t1 = time.time()\n    # ---- neural -------------------------------------------------------------\n    fr = bin_spikes(d['unit_spikes'], win_start)          # (n_units, n_trials, 80)\n    fr = fr[:, trial_idx, :]\n    # drop units that are completely silent in every extracted window\n    active = fr.any(axis=(1, 2))\n    fr = fr[active]\n    unit_region = d['unit_region'][active]\n    t_neural = time.time() - t1\n\n    # ---- inputs -------------------------------------------------------------\n    t2 = time.time()\n    tone_rel = tone_onset_rel(d)[trial_idx]               # <= 0\n    time_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS[None, :] - tone_rel[:, None]     # (n_tr, 80)\n\n    on, off = photostim_windows(d)\n    on, off = on[trial_idx], off[trial_idx]\n    has_stim = np.isfinite(on)\n    # A bin is \"on\" when the laser is on at the bin centre, i.e. the binary\n    # laser signal is sampled at the same instants as the bin centres.  Using\n    # interval overlap instead would light up the bin containing the go cue for\n    # a third of the stimulation trials, because the recorded laser-off\n    # timestamp overshoots the go cue by ~1 ms; the photoinhibition always ended\n    # before the go cue (datapaper STAR Methods).  With the centre rule each\n    # 0.5 s stimulation covers exactly 10 bins.\n    stim = np.zeros((len(trial_idx), N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n    if has_stim.any():\n        o = np.where(has_stim, on, np.inf)[:, None]\n        f_ = np.where(has_stim, off, -np.inf)[:, None]\n        stim = ((BIN_CENTERS[None, :] >= o) & (BIN_CENTERS[None, :] <= f_)\n                ).astype(np.float32)\n\n    # ---- outputs ------------------------------------------------------------\n    choice = choice_from_trials(d)[trial_idx]\n    outcome_map = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n    outcome = np.array([outcome_map[o] for o in d['outcome']])[trial_idx]\n    early = (d['early_lick'] == 'early').astype(np.int64)[trial_idx]\n    tongue, p_lo, p_hi = tongue_classes(d, win_start[trial_idx])\n    t_io = time.time() - t2\n\n    # ---- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n    t3 = time.time()\n    n_tr = len(trial_idx)\n    neural = [np.ascontiguousarray(fr[:, i, :]) for i in range(n_tr)]\n    inputs = [np.stack([time_from_tone[i], stim[i]]).astype(np.float32)\n              for i in range(n_tr)]\n    outputs = [np.stack([np.full(N_BINS, choice[i]), np.full(N_BINS, outcome[i]),\n                         np.full(N_BINS, early[i]), tongue[i]]).astype(np.int64)\n               for i in range(n_tr)]\n    t_pack = time.time() - t3\n\n    # provenance: row indices into intervals/trials of the source NWB file, one\n    # per converted trial, so every value can be traced back to the raw data\n    info['raw_trial_index'] = d['ephys_trials'][trial_idx].astype(int).tolist()\n    info.update(n_trials=n_tr, n_neurons=int(fr.shape[0]),\n                tongue_p40=float(p_lo), tongue_p60=float(p_hi),\n                n_photostim_trials=int(has_stim.sum()),\n                t_read=t_read, t_neural=t_neural, t_io=t_io, t_pack=t_pack,\n                t_total=time.time() - t0)\n\n    result = dict(session_id=d['session_id'], subject=d['subject'],\n                  neural=neural, input=inputs, output=outputs,\n                  unit_region=unit_region, info=info)\n\n    if want_debug:\n        result['debug'] = dict(\n            go=go, trial_idx=trial_idx, win_start=win_start,\n            tone_rel=tone_rel, stim_on=on, stim_off=off,\n            tongue_t=d['tongue_t'], tongue_y=d['tongue_y'], tongue_lik=d['tongue_lik'],\n            p_lo=p_lo, p_hi=p_hi, choice=choice, outcome=outcome, early=early,\n            unit_anno=d['unit_anno'][active],\n            trial_start=d['trial_start'], trial_stop=d['trial_stop'],\n        )\n    return result, info\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------------ plotting\ndef make_processing_plot(res, outpath):\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    dbg = res['debug']\n    neural = res['neural']\n    inputs = res['input']\n    outputs = res['output']\n    n_tr = len(neural)\n    n_neu = neural[0].shape[0]\n\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(4, 3, figsize=(22, 18))\n\n    # (0,0) trial-averaged population firing rate + epoch markers\n    mean_fr = np.mean([n.mean(axis=0) for n in neural], axis=0)\n    ax[0, 0].plot(BIN_CENTERS, mean_fr, '-o', ms=3)\n    ax[0, 0].axvline(0, color='r', label='go cue')\n    med_tone = np.median(dbg['tone_rel'])\n    ax[0, 0].axvline(med_tone, color='g', ls='--', label='median tone onset')\n    ax[0, 0].axvline(med_tone + 0.65, color='g', ls=':', label='sample end')\n    ax[0, 0].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n    ax[0, 0].set_ylabel('mean firing rate (Hz)')\n    ax[0, 0].set_title(f'{res[\"session_id\"]}: population PSTH ({n_neu} neurons, {n_tr} trials)')\n    ax[0, 0].legend(fontsize=7)\n\n    # (0,1) fraction of bins with zero spikes across the population (coverage)\n    frac_zero = np.mean([(n.sum(axis=0) == 0) for n in neural], axis=0)\n    ax[0, 1].plot(BIN_CENTERS, frac_zero, '-o', ms=3)\n    ax[0, 1].axvline(0, color='r')\n    ax[0, 1].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n    ax[0, 1].set_ylabel('fraction of trials with no spikes')\n    ax[0, 1].set_title('trial-interval coverage\\n(rises late: error trials end early)')\n\n    # (0,2) raster-like heatmap of one trial\n    it = int(np.argmax([o[1, 0] == 2 for o in outputs]))   # a hit trial\n    im = ax[0, 2].imshow(neural[it], aspect='auto', interpolation='nearest',\n                         extent=[T_START, T_STOP, n_neu, 0], cmap='magma')\n    ax[0, 2].axvline(0, color='c')\n    ax[0, 2].set_title(f'trial {it}: neural (Hz)')\n    ax[0, 2].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n    ax[0, 2].set_ylabel('neuron')\n    plt.colorbar(im, ax=ax[0, 2])\n\n    # (1,0) input 0: time from tone onset\n    for i in range(min(40, n_tr)):\n        ax[1, 0].plot(BIN_CENTERS, inputs[i][0], alpha=0.4)\n    ax[1, 0].axvline(0, color='r')\n    ax[1, 0].axhline(0, color='k', ls=':')\n    ax[1, 0].set_title('input 0: time from tone onset (s)\\n(0 crossing = tone onset)')\n    ax[1, 0].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n\n    # (1,1) input 1: photostim raster\n    stim = np.stack([inp[1] for inp in inputs])\n    ax[1, 1].imshow(stim, aspect='auto', interpolation='nearest',\n                    extent=[T_START, T_STOP, n_tr, 0], cmap='Greys')\n    ax[1, 1].axvline(0, color='r')\n    ax[1, 1].set_title(f'input 1: photostim on ({int((stim.max(axis=1) > 0).sum())} trials)')\n    ax[1, 1].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n    ax[1, 1].set_ylabel('trial')\n\n    # (1,2) check photostim against raw event times\n    ok = np.isfinite(dbg['stim_on'])\n    if ok.any():\n        ax[1, 2].hist(dbg['stim_on'][ok], bins=40, alpha=0.6, label='laser on')\n        ax[1, 2].hist(dbg['stim_off'][ok], bins=40, alpha=0.6, label='laser off')\n    ax[1, 2].axvline(0, color='r', label='go cue')\n    ax[1, 2].set_title('raw photostim event times re. go cue')\n    ax[1, 2].set_xlabel('s'); ax[1, 2].legend(fontsize=7)\n\n    # (2,0) tongue y trace for one trial, raw frames + derived classes\n    itt = int(np.argmax([(o[3] != 3).sum() for o in outputs]))\n    t0 = dbg['win_start'][dbg['trial_idx'][itt]]\n    m = (dbg['tongue_t'] >= t0) & (dbg['tongue_t'] < t0 + N_BINS * BIN_WIDTH)\n    tt = dbg['tongue_t'][m] - (t0 - T_START)\n    yy = dbg['tongue_y'][m]\n    vis = dbg['tongue_lik'][m] > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH\n    ax[2, 0].plot(tt[vis], yy[vis], '.', ms=2, label='visible frames')\n    ax[2, 0].axhline(dbg['p_lo'], color='g', ls='--', label='40th pct')\n    ax[2, 0].axhline(dbg['p_hi'], color='m', ls='--', label='60th pct')\n    ax2 = ax[2, 0].twinx()\n    ax2.step(BIN_CENTERS, outputs[itt][3], where='mid', color='k', alpha=0.5)\n    ax2.set_ylabel('class (0-3)')\n    ax[2, 0].axvline(0, color='r')\n    ax[2, 0].set_title(f'trial {itt}: raw tongue y vs derived class')\n    ax[2, 0].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[2, 0].legend(fontsize=7)\n\n    # (2,1) tongue class raster\n    tong = np.stack([o[3] for o in outputs])\n    im = ax[2, 1].imshow(tong, aspect='auto', interpolation='nearest',\n                         extent=[T_START, T_STOP, n_tr, 0], cmap='viridis', vmin=0, vmax=3)\n    ax[2, 1].axvline(0, color='r')\n    ax[2, 1].set_title('output 3: tongue y class (3 = not visible)')\n    ax[2, 1].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[2, 1].set_ylabel('trial')\n    plt.colorbar(im, ax=ax[2, 1])\n\n    # (2,2) class fractions over time\n    for c, lab in enumerate(OUTPUT_VALUES[3]):\n        ax[2, 2].plot(BIN_CENTERS, (tong == c).mean(axis=0), label=lab)\n    ax[2, 2].axvline(0, color='r')\n    ax[2, 2].set_title('tongue class fraction over time\\n(tongue appears after the go cue)')\n    ax[2, 2].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[2, 2].legend(fontsize=7)\n\n    # (3,0) per-trial outputs\n    for k, name in enumerate(OUTPUT_NAMES[:3]):\n        vals = np.array([o[k, 0] for o in outputs])\n        ax[3, 0].plot(vals + 0.1 * k, '.', ms=2, label=name)\n    ax[3, 0].set_title('per-trial outputs across the session')\n    ax[3, 0].set_xlabel('trial'); ax[3, 0].legend(fontsize=7)\n\n    # (3,1) output distributions\n    labels, fracs = [], []\n    for k in range(4):\n        vals = np.concatenate([o[k] for o in outputs])\n        for c, lab in enumerate(OUTPUT_VALUES[k]):\n            labels.append(f'{OUTPUT_NAMES[k]}={lab}')\n            fracs.append((vals == c).mean())\n    ax[3, 1].barh(np.arange(len(fracs)), fracs)\n    ax[3, 1].set_yticks(np.arange(len(fracs)))\n    ax[3, 1].set_yticklabels(labels, fontsize=7)\n    ax[3, 1].set_title('output class fractions (time-point weighted)')\n\n    # (3,2) PSTH split by choice\n    ch = np.array([o[0, 0] for o in outputs])\n    for c, lab in enumerate(OUTPUT_VALUES[0]):\n        m = ch == c\n        if m.sum() == 0:\n            continue\n        ax[3, 2].plot(BIN_CENTERS, np.mean([neural[i].mean(axis=0)\n                                            for i in np.where(m)[0]], axis=0), label=lab)\n    ax[3, 2].axvline(0, color='r')\n    ax[3, 2].set_title('population PSTH by choice')\n    ax[3, 2].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)'); ax[3, 2].legend(fontsize=7)\n\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    fig.savefig(outpath, dpi=110)\n    plt.close(fig)\n    print(f'  wrote {outpath}')\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- main\ndef _worker(args):\n    path, want_debug = args\n    try:\n        return process_session(path, want_debug=want_debug)\n    except Exception as e:  # keep one bad file from killing the whole run\n        import traceback\n        traceback.print_exc()\n        return None, dict(session_id=session_id_from_path(path), path=path,\n                          rejected=f'error: {e}')\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,\n                                 formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)\n    ap.add_argument('outfile')\n    g = ap.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\n    g.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True)\n    g.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true')\n    ap.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true')\n    ap.add_argument('--jobs', type=int, default=min(32, os.cpu_count() or 8))\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA_DIR, 'sub-*', '*.nwb')))\n    print(f'found {len(files)} NWB files')\n    n_sample = 2\n    if args.sample:\n        # keep scanning until `n_sample` sessions pass the session-selection\n        # criteria, so the sample is a usable mini-dataset\n        files = files[:8]\n        print(f'--sample: scanning up to {len(files)} files for '\n              f'{n_sample} accepted sessions')\n\n    # `--show-processing` plots the first two *accepted* sessions, so debug\n    # information is requested for a few more files than that.\n    n_debug = 8 if args.show_processing else 0\n    jobs = [(p, i < n_debug) for i, p in enumerate(files)]\n    n_plots = 0\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    results, infos = [], []\n    nworkers = max(1, min(args.jobs, len(files)))\n    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=nworkers) as ex:\n        for k, (res, info) in enumerate(ex.map(_worker, jobs)):\n            if args.sample and len(results) >= n_sample:\n                break\n            infos.append(info)\n            if res is None:\n                print(f'[{k+1}/{len(files)}] SKIP {info[\"session_id\"]}: '\n                      f'{info.get(\"rejected\")} (perf={info.get(\"performance\", float(\"nan\")):.3f}, '\n                      f'L={info.get(\"n_correct_left\")}, R={info.get(\"n_correct_right\")})',\n                      flush=True)\n                continue\n            results.append(res)\n            i = res['info']\n            print(f'[{k+1}/{len(files)}] {i[\"session_id\"]}: {i[\"n_neurons\"]} neurons, '\n                  f'{i[\"n_trials\"]} trials, perf={i[\"performance\"]:.3f}, '\n                  f'{i[\"t_total\"]:.1f}s (read {i[\"t_read\"]:.1f} / bin {i[\"t_neural\"]:.1f} '\n                  f'/ io {i[\"t_io\"]:.2f} / pack {i[\"t_pack\"]:.2f})', flush=True)\n            if 'debug' in res:\n                if n_plots < 2:\n                    make_processing_plot(res, f'/app/processing_{res[\"session_id\"]}.png')\n                    n_plots += 1\n                del res['debug']\n\n    t_proc = time.time() - t0\n    print(f'\\nprocessed {len(results)}/{len(files)} sessions in {t_proc:.1f}s '\n          f'({t_proc / max(1, len(files)):.2f}s per file, {nworkers} workers)')\n\n    # A series of three '\n                '150 ms pure tones (3 kHz -> lick right, 12 kHz -> lick left) is played '\n                'during a 0.65 s sample epoch, followed by a 1.2 s delay epoch during '\n                'which the mouse must withhold licking. Additionally, the AI applies **session-level curation** based on the data paper's STAR Methods: sessions must have behavioral performance > 65% on control non-early-lick trials, and at least 50 correct lick-left and 50 correct lick-right trials. A session with degenerate tongue tracking (>50% frames visible) is also rejected. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials come from the NWB trials table (`intervals/trials`). The AI first restricts to trials covered by the electrophysiology recording using `units/obs_intervals`, then applies further trial-level filters.\n\nii.\n```python\noi_index = u['obs_intervals_index'][:]\nn_int = np.diff(np.concatenate([[0], oi_index]))\nassert n_int.min() == n_int.max(), 'units disagree on observed trials'\nobs = u['obs_intervals'][0:oi_index[0]]\nephys_trials = np.searchsorted(trial_start_all, obs[:, 0])\nassert np.allclose(trial_start_all[ephys_trials], obs[:, 0])\n```\n\niii. Three trial-level filters are applied:\n1. **obs_intervals**: Trials not covered by the electrophysiology recording are excluded (handled at read time by restricting to `ephys_trials`).\n2. **auto_water and free_water**: Both are excluded because reward is not contingent on the animal's choice, so `outcome` is not a behavioral report.\n3. **Video coverage**: Trials where the video covers less than 90% of the [-2.5, +1.5]s window are dropped (254 trials across the dataset).\n\nPhotostimulation, early-lick, and no-response (ignore) trials are kept because they are required decoder inputs/outputs.\n\nii.\n```python\nkeep = (~d['auto_water']) & (~d['free_water'])\ninfo['n_dropped_water'] = int((~keep).sum())\ncov = video_coverage(d, win_start)\nkeep &= cov >= MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE\ninfo['n_dropped_video'] = int(((cov < MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE)).sum())\ntrial_idx = np.where(keep)[0]\n```\n\niii. Final trial count: 70,949 across 136 sessions.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `units/spike_times` (ragged spike time arrays) for units passing quality control. Go-cue times from `BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` define the bin edges.\n\nii.\n```python\nst_index = u['spike_times_index'][:]\nstarts = np.concatenate([[0], st_index[:-1]])\nspike_times = u['spike_times'][:]\nunit_spikes = [spike_times[starts[i]:st_index[i]] for i in idx]\n```\n\niii. `spike_times` is the only neural representation in the NWB files. The AI reads all spike times at once and slices per unit, rather than reading per-unit.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 80 non-overlapping 50ms bins spanning [-2.5, +1.5)s relative to the go cue. Spike counts per bin are divided by the bin width (0.05s) to yield firing rates in Hz. No smoothing, normalization, or baseline subtraction is applied.\n\nThe AI uses a vectorized single-pass approach: all spikes from all units are concatenated, their trial index is found by one `np.searchsorted` over the window-start array, their bin index by integer division, and the full (unit, trial, bin) tensor is built with one `np.bincount`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef bin_spikes(unit_spikes, win_start):\n    t = np.concatenate(unit_spikes)\n    uidx = np.repeat(np.arange(n_units), lens)\n    ti = np.searchsorted(win_start, t, side='right') - 1\n    # ... validity checks ...\n    b = ((t - win_start[ti]) / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.int64)\n    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n    flat = (uidx * n_trials + ti) * N_BINS + b\n    counts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_trials * N_BINS)\n    return (counts / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. Firing rate in Hz matches the reference code's `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` which returns `binSpikes / bin_width`. The vectorized bincount approach is O(n_spikes) instead of per-unit per-trial searchsorted.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Three unit-level filters:\n1. `units/classification == 'good'` (the QC classifier verdict from the spike-sorting white paper).\n2. The unit's CCF annotation (`anno_name`) must map to one of 14 major brain regions via a `region_map.py` module.\n3. After computing firing rates, units that are completely silent across all extracted windows are dropped.\n\nii.\n```python\nclassification = _decode(u['classification'][:])\nanno = _decode(u['anno_name'][:])\ngood = classification == 'good'\nregions = np.array([annotation_to_region(a) or '' for a in anno])\nkeep_unit = good & (regions != '')\n# ...\n# after binning:\nactive = fr.any(axis=(1, 2))\nfr = fr[active]\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES state that all 293 distinct annotations of good units map successfully, so the CCF filter does not actually remove any units in practice. The activity filter matches the intent of the reference code's `check_fr` (drops neurons with zero across-trial variance). The AI reports 54,629 good units across 136 kept sessions, median 392 per session vs the paper's 393.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. The go-cue time from `BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` serves as time zero. Bin edges are computed as `go_time + T_START` to `go_time + T_STOP` in absolute session time. All NWB timestamps share a single session-absolute clock, so no interpolation or offset correction is needed.\n\nii.\n```python\ngo = d['go_time']\nwin_start = go + T_START\n# in bin_spikes:\nti = np.searchsorted(win_start, t, side='right') - 1\nb = ((t - win_start[ti]) / BIN_WIDTH).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The reference code also aligns everything to the go cue. The AI verifies that trial windows never overlap (go cues are >= 4.58s apart).\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. 50ms non-overlapping bins, 80 bins spanning [-2.5, +1.5)s relative to the go cue. This matches the task specification. No rebinning is applied since spikes are binned directly from raw spike times.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_WIDTH = 0.05\nT_START = -2.5\nT_STOP = 1.5\nN_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN_WIDTH))   # 80\nBIN_EDGES = T_START + BIN_WIDTH * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\nBIN_CENTERS = BIN_EDGES[:-1] + BIN_WIDTH / 2\n```\n\niii. The reference paper used 40ms bins with 3.4ms stride, but the task specifies 50ms bins. The AI notes this as a required deviation.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. From `BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times` (tone onset timestamps) and the go-cue time. The tone for a trial is the **last** sample-epoch onset at or before the go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef tone_onset_rel(d):\n    si = np.searchsorted(d['sample_start'], d['go_time'], side='right') - 1\n    assert (si >= 0).all(), 'a trial has no preceding sample epoch'\n    return d['sample_start'][si] - d['go_time']\n```\n\niii. Early-lick trials replay the sample epoch, so there can be multiple sample onsets per trial. The last one before the go cue is the relevant one.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. For each trial, the value at each bin center is: `bin_center_relative_to_go - tone_onset_relative_to_go`. Since `tone_onset_rel` returns a negative value (tone is before go), `bin_center - tone_rel` gives seconds since the tone.\n\nii.\n```python\ntone_rel = tone_onset_rel(d)[trial_idx]\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS[None, :] - tone_rel[:, None]\n```\n\niii. This is a continuous, time-varying input. For standard trials the tone is at -1.85s relative to the go cue, so the value at the go-cue bin is 1.875s (bin center at 0.025s).\n\n## 3-c. How is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The `BIN_CENTERS` array is defined relative to the go cue, the same event the neural data is aligned to. So the time-from-tone value at bin k corresponds to the same 50ms interval as the neural data at bin k.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_CENTERS = BIN_EDGES[:-1] + BIN_WIDTH / 2\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS[None, :] - tone_rel[:, None]\n```\n\niii. Both neural and input share the same bin grid, ensuring alignment.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Photostimulation* derived from?\n\ni. From `BehavioralEvents/photostim_start_times` and `photostim_stop_times` (the actual recorded laser on/off timestamps in session time). These are mapped to trials by `np.searchsorted` against trial start times, then expressed relative to the go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\nd['stim_on'] = ev['photostim_start_times']['timestamps'][:]\nd['stim_off'] = ev['photostim_stop_times']['timestamps'][:]\n# ...\ndef photostim_windows(d):\n    ti = np.searchsorted(d['trial_start'], d['stim_on'], side='right') - 1\n    on[ti] = s_on - d['go_time'][ti]\n    off[ti] = s_off - d['go_time'][ti]\n    return on, off\n```\n\niii. The AI uses the event-level timestamps rather than the trials table fields (`photostim_onset`/`photostim_duration`). The AI documents that this avoids issues with the laser-off timestamp overshooting the go cue by ~1ms.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. A binary time series: 1.0 where the bin center falls within the [on, off] interval, 0.0 elsewhere. The AI uses `>=` for the onset and `<=` for the offset (closed interval).\n\nii.\n```python\no = np.where(has_stim, on, np.inf)[:, None]\nf_ = np.where(has_stim, off, -np.inf)[:, None]\nstim = ((BIN_CENTERS[None, :] >= o) & (BIN_CENTERS[None, :] <= f_)).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. Non-stimulated trials get inf/neg-inf bounds so the condition is always False. The AI notes each 0.5s stimulation covers exactly 10 bins (matching the data paper).\n\n## 4-c. How is `input` *Photostimulation* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The photostim on/off times are expressed relative to the go cue (same as the bin centers), so the binary trace is automatically aligned with the neural bins.\n\nii.\n```python\non[ti] = s_on - d['go_time'][ti]\noff[ti] = s_off - d['go_time'][ti]\n# compared against BIN_CENTERS which are go-cue-relative\n```\n\niii. Same alignment mechanism as all other streams.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction choice* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from two trials-table columns: `outcome` (hit/miss/ignore) and `trial_instruction` (left/right). Choice is not stored directly in the NWB file.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef choice_from_trials(d):\n    ch = np.full(len(d['outcome']), 2, dtype=np.int64)\n    hit = d['outcome'] == 'hit'\n    miss = d['outcome'] == 'miss'\n    left = d['instruction'] == 'left'\n    ch[hit & left] = 0\n    ch[hit & ~left] = 1\n    ch[miss & left] = 1\n    ch[miss & ~left] = 0\n    return ch\n```\n\niii. Hit means the animal licked the instructed side; miss means the opposite side; ignore means no lick. The AI cross-validated this derivation against actual lick timestamps (99.92% agreement).\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction choice*?\n\ni. Mapped to integers: 0=left, 1=right, 2=no lick. The per-trial value is broadcast across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\noutputs = [np.stack([np.full(N_BINS, choice[i]), np.full(N_BINS, outcome[i]),\n                     np.full(N_BINS, early[i]), tongue[i]]).astype(np.int64)\n           for i in range(n_tr)]\n```\n\niii. Broadcasting ensures all four outputs share a single (4, 80) array per trial.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 80 non-overlapping 50ms bins spanning [-2.5, +1.5)s relative to the go cue. Spike counts per bin are divided by the bin width (0.05s) to yield firing rates in Hz.
